🔶 ⚠️ **The Indian women's movement.** The **legal-reform and political-representation campaigns from the 1970s onward** are **a standard illustrative anchor for "formal legal equality is not the end of the feminist question"**. ⚠️ Use it **as a *type* of example (movement-led legal reform), not with specific statute names, years or case citations unless separately verified through a dedicated Polity/legal source**.

⚠️ **This anchor is the best-fitting of the three**, because it instantiates the chapter's central thesis exactly. ⚠️ Lesson 16's claim is that **even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life**; a movement that continues after formal equality has been granted is that claim in historical form. ⚠️ And the dating aligns: the chapter places the shift of attention **from 1960s and 1970s** and the Women's Liberation Movement in the **early 1970s**, so the Indian campaigns **from the 1970s onward** are contemporaneous rather than derivative.

### 3. 🔶 The governing rule

🔶 ⚠️ **The owner states the division of labour explicitly, and it is the rule for the whole lesson:** this file owns the **conceptual** application — **patriarchy, sex/gender, representation gap** — and **does not own specific constitutional articles, acts or case law**; those must be **routed to the Polity module** rather than asserted here. ⚠️ The advanced companion adds the complementary prohibition: ❌ **do not turn this into a current-data note; the assigned task is political theory, not present-day gender indicators**.

⚠️ **The practical test before writing any Indian sentence** is therefore: is this a **concept** or a **legal/empirical fact**? ⚠️ Concepts — patriarchy, the sex/gender distinction, the representation gap, protection versus empowerment — may be applied freely. ❌ Articles, sections, years, case names, percentages and current figures may not be asserted from this topic at all.

### 4. 🔶 The names neither source carries

⚠️ ⚠️ **Two names are routinely expected in wider feminist syllabi and appear in **neither** the chapter nor either owner, and the honest course is to declare the absence rather than to fill it silently.**

| Name | ⚠️ Status in this topic's sources | ⚠️ What may be said |
|---|---|---|
| **Friedrich Engels**, *The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State*, **1884** ⚠️ (title and year independently verified in this session) | ❌ Not named in PDF pp.84-93; ❌ not named in either canonical owner. ⚠️ The owner's Marxist-feminism subsection discusses the strand without naming him | ⚠️ May be named as a standard reference **outside** this topic's sources, with the absence declared. ❌ No argument, thesis or claim may be attributed to him from here. ➡️ Marxism's apparatus belongs to Topic 04 |
| **Betty Friedan**, *The Feminine Mystique*, **1963** ⚠️ (title and year independently verified in this session) | ❌ Not named in PDF pp.84-93; ❌ not named in either canonical owner. ⚠️ The chapter does name **National Organization for Women (NOW)** at Lesson 14 without naming any individual | ⚠️ May be named as a standard reference **outside** this topic's sources, with the absence declared. ❌ No thesis may be attributed to her from here |

⚠️ ❌ **The discipline, stated plainly.** ⚠️ Where an answer genuinely needs either name, the correct move is to name the work and year — both verified above — and to attribute the substantive claim to an independent source, not to this chapter or its owners. ❌ Reconstructing either argument from general memory and presenting it as part of this topic's material is exactly the failure this session is built to prevent. ⚠️ Declaring an absence costs nothing and is itself evidence of source discipline.

Revision notes:
- 🔶 ⚠️ Entire lesson is outside PDF pp.84-93, from the owner's §15; Lesson 15 is the separate, page-anchored Indian material.
- 🔶 **Savitribai Phule** — **widely associated, with Jyotirao Phule, with pioneering girls' education in nineteenth-century Maharashtra**; ceiling: **early advocate of women's education against caste-and-gender exclusion**. ❌ No school-opening dates or numbers.
- ⚠️ Her value: shows the educational deprivation of Lesson 8 being contested in India in the same century, and introduces a **caste-and-gender** compound the chapter has no axis for.
- 🔶 **B. R. Ambedkar** — **widely associated with linking women's legal and social equality to the wider project of social democracy, including reform of Hindu personal law**; treat as **a general, well-known orientation**. ❌ No bill provisions, sections or dates.
- 🔶 **The Indian women's movement**, **from the 1970s onward** — a standard anchor for **formal legal equality is not the end of the feminist question**; use **as a type of example (movement-led legal reform)**. ❌ No statute names, years or case citations.
- ⚠️ It instantiates Lesson 16's thesis exactly, and is contemporaneous with the chapter's **from 1960s and 1970s** dating.
- 🔶 Governing rule: this topic owns the **conceptual** application and **does not own specific constitutional articles, acts or case law**. ➡️ Polity. ❌ And it is not a current-data note.
- ❌ **Engels** (*The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State*, **1884**) and **Friedan** (*The Feminine Mystique*, **1963**) — ⚠️ titles and years verified, but **neither is named** in the chapter or in either owner. ⚠️ Declare the absence; ❌ attribute no claim to them from this topic.

### Concept check

**Question:** You want to use Savitribai Phule in a 15-mark answer on feminism and education. What may you assert, what must you not, and what is the analytical payoff?

**Model answer:** 🔶 What may be asserted is that she is **widely associated, with Jyotirao Phule, with pioneering girls' education in nineteenth-century Maharashtra**, used as **an illustration of an early, home-grown challenge to gendered exclusion from education**, with the claim kept at the owner's ceiling — **early advocate of women's education against caste-and-gender exclusion**. ❌ What must not be asserted is any **specific unverified school-opening dates or numbers**, which the owner forbids unless independently confirmed, and by extension any institutional detail, enrolment figure or chronology; the governing rule is that this topic owns the **conceptual** application and **does not own specific constitutional articles, acts or case law**. ⚠️ The analytical payoff is twofold and does not require a single date. First, it answers a question the chapter's exclusively Western references leave open: Lesson 8 named **opportunities of education and higher learning** as a deprivation with **no logical connection with her biological status as a woman**, and Lesson 9 recorded the nineteenth-century agenda as **removing educational and professional barriers** — Phule shows the same deprivation being contested in India in the same century, so the challenge was not imported. ⚠️ Second, and more valuable, the owner's safe ceiling names **caste-and-gender exclusion**, a compound axis the chapter has no vocabulary for, since Gauba differentiates women only by country. ⚠️ That is Lesson 22's intersectional correction in an Indian register, and it lets an answer demonstrate the most sophisticated move available in the topic using an example whose factual exposure is nil.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not supply Engels or Friedan as part of this topic's material. Neither is named in PDF pp.84-93 or in either canonical owner — the chapter names **National Organization for Women (NOW)** without naming any individual, and the owner discusses Marxist feminism without naming Engels. Their works and years may be stated (*The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State*, 1884; *The Feminine Mystique*, 1963, both verified in this session), but any substantive claim must be attributed to an independent source and the absence from this topic's sources declared.

## Lesson 24 — Separating the schools; answer architecture

Progress: 24 / 24 | Stage: Advanced | Subtopic: The discriminating questions, the standing checks, and the 10/15/20-mark structures

━━━ PRE-TEACH CHECKLIST ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Book context: Canonical Core owner §§11 and 16 (revision capsule, directive decoding and the three mark-level architectures) with the Advanced companion's §§9 and 10 (application without current-affairs dependence, and the answer-engine prompts), read against the whole of the source chapter's range (Gauba, Ch.2, PDF pp.84-93) as the evidence base the architectures draw on.
CA search: Not re-run for this lesson; see Lesson 1.
CA found: As reported there.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

🖼️ **VISUAL — four questions that separate every school in the topic**

```text
   Q1  WHERE IS THE OBSTACLE LOCATED?
   +-------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | LIBERAL           | formal barriers, discriminatory law        |
   | RADICAL           | patriarchal sexual power + reproduction    |
   | SOCIALIST         | patriarchy AND capitalism, together        |
   | 🔶 MARXIST        | the capitalist mode of production, chiefly |
   +-------------------+-------------------------------------------+

   Q2  WHO BENEFITS?
   +-------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | LIBERAL           | (not posed as a beneficiary question)      |
   | RADICAL           | men                                        |
   | SOCIALIST         | "CAPITALISTS AS WELL AS MEN"               |
   | 🔶 MARXIST        | capital, principally                       |
   +-------------------+-------------------------------------------+

   Q3  WHAT WOULD COUNT AS SUCCESS?
   +-------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | LIBERAL           | "ABSOLUTE EQUALITY OF OPPORTUNITY"         |
   | RADICAL           | Firestone: care "DIFFUSED TO SOCIETY AS A  |
   |                   |   WHOLE"; Millett: "SMASH THESE IMPLEMENTS"|
   | SOCIALIST         | success "ONLY THROUGH COMBINING THESE      |
   |                   |   STRUGGLES"                               |
   +-------------------+-------------------------------------------+

   Q4  WHICH WOMEN DOES THE CLAIM DESCRIBE?   🔶 (Lesson 22)
   -> the question NONE of the three chapter schools asks, and the
      one intersectionality forces. Asking it is the single
      highest-value move in a 20-mark answer.

   ===================================================================
   FOUR STANDING CHECKS -- run before writing a single line
   ===================================================================
   1. 📰 DATE CHECK   Is this figure from 1980 / the late 20th
      century / HDR 1995 / 2009? Attach the period. NEVER "today".
   2. 🔶 PROVENANCE   Is this from PDF pp.84-93, or from the
      owner's extension (Beauvoir, Oakley, Rubin, Marxist
      feminism, Pateman, Okin, Butler, Crenshaw, Phule,
      Ambedkar)? Say which.
   3. ❌ BOUNDARY     No constitutional article, section, case,
      amendment number or current statistic. Route to Polity.
   4. ⚠️ HOMOGENEITY  Have I written "women" as if they were one
      undifferentiated group? If so, apply Q4.
```

*Caption: four questions separate the schools, and four checks protect the answer. Together they are the whole topic in a form that survives exam conditions.*

### 1. The discriminating questions

⚠️ **Q1 and Q3 are enough for a 10-mark comparison; Q2 is what distinguishes a 15-mark answer; Q4 is what distinguishes a 20-mark one.**

⚠️ **Q2 repays particular attention because it is the cleanest single discriminator.** ⚠️ The chapter's own sentence does the work: socialist feminism **implies that capitalists as well as men are the beneficiary of women's subordination**. ⚠️ Identifying **two** beneficiaries is what makes it a dual-system position; identifying **one** is what makes radical feminism radical and 🔶 Marxist feminism reductive. ⚠️ No other question separates the three so economically.

⚠️ **Q4 is the question the chapter never asks.** ⚠️ Gauba writes of **women** throughout and differentiates them only by country, at Lessons 17 and 19. 🔶 Crenshaw's correction — ask **which** women a claim describes — is therefore not an addition to the chapter but a repair of a structural silence in it. ⚠️ Raising it, with its limit attached, is the most reliable way to lift an answer into the top band.

### 2. Fast separations

| Confusion | ⚠️ The one-line separation |
|---|---|
| **Sex** vs **gender** | **Nature has divided human race between men and women, but their status and role in society are determined by our culture.** |
| **Natural** vs **conventional** inequality | Natural is **largely unalterable**; conventional is **the product of our social arrangements** and therefore **alterable** |
| **Liberal** vs **radical** | Barriers versus **power**; **absolute equality of opportunity** versus **smash these implements** |
| **Radical** vs **socialist** | One beneficiary versus **capitalists as well as men** |
| 🔶 **Socialist** vs **Marxist** | Dual systems versus **principally** the capitalist mode of production — **the two labels overlap but are not simple synonyms** |
| **Firestone** vs **Millett** | **Why** (reproductive biology) versus **what** (**power … sustained by an ideology**) |
| **View (a)** vs **view (b)** on equality | **Governed by the same laws** versus **equal opportunities to develop and apply their distinctive capabilities along with equal rights** |
| **GDI** vs **GEM** | Health and education versus **the extent to which women participate in a country's economic and political life** |
| **Protection** vs **empowerment** | Lesson 15's own sort, and Lesson 8's **security but not an opportunity** |
| **Legal equality** vs **subjection** | **Even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life** |

### 3. The four standing checks, in detail

📰 ⚠️ **Check 1 — date.** ⚠️ Every empirical claim in this chapter is dated. The United Nations figure is **1980**; the legislature percentages and the Charter of Paris are late twentieth century; the indices are **Human Development Report, 1995**; the Indian statutes run to **2005** and are called **recent** in a **2009** edition; the heads-of-state list ends at **2007**. ❌ The sentence **the condition remains more or less unchanged today** must never be reproduced as a present-tense claim. ⚠️ The safe formula is to cite the **shape** of a finding with its period attached.

🔶 ⚠️ **Check 2 — provenance.** ⚠️ Three tiers, and an answer should always be able to say which it is in. **Tier 1** is PDF pp.84-93, cited by page. **Tier 2** is the owner's extension — Beauvoir, Oakley, Rubin, Marxist feminism, Pateman, Okin, Butler, Crenshaw, Phule, Ambedkar, the Indian women's movement — marked 🔶 and never cited to Gauba. **Tier 3** is outside both, and the only Tier-3 names this session uses are **Engels** and **Friedan**, whose absence is declared rather than filled.

❌ ⚠️ **Check 3 — boundary.** ❌ No constitutional article, section, amendment number, case citation, legislative history or current statistic. ➡️ Polity for law, ➡️ the citizenship and justice owners for those concepts, ➡️ Topic 04 for Marxism's apparatus, ➡️ the power and authority owner for Weber. ⚠️ The chapter's own Indian paragraph at Lesson 15 is the **only** legal material available, and it is usable only as short titles with years and with the 2009 vantage attached.

⚠️ **Check 4 — homogeneity.** ⚠️ This is the check specific to this topic and it is the one most often failed. ⚠️ Before submitting, scan for any sentence that treats **women** as a single undifferentiated class with uniform interests. 🔶 Where one is found, apply Crenshaw's question — **which** women? — with its limit, that intersectionality **supplements, but should not replace, the structural claims** of the schools.

### 4. The three architectures

⚠️ **10 marks (~150 words).** Thesis in one or two lines — feminism protests patriarchal subordination via the sex/gender distinction, and formal legal equality alone is insufficient. Then the sex/gender distinction with the hinge sentence, **one** stream with its core mechanism, **one** qualifier or limit, and a one-line conclusion tied to the thesis. ⚠️ At this length cite **one** dated illustration at most, and cite it with its date.

⚠️ **15 marks (~200-250 words).** Thesis with directive-specific framing. Then the doctrine: the sex/gender distinction, 🔶 with Beauvoir/Oakley/Rubin flagged as provenance if the question invites it, and patriarchy as a political rather than merely domestic category. Then **two or three** streams, each as claim → evidence → significance → limit. Then **one** objection-reply chain. Then **one** cautious Indian illustration at its ceiling. Then a reasoned conclusion. ⚠️ The **limit** clause on each stream is what the band depends on.

⚠️ **20 marks (~250-300 words).** Thesis plus explicit directive decoding. Full doctrine: patriarchy → sex/gender → why biology does not justify hierarchy, using Rousseau's natural/conventional distinction as the chapter does. **All** streams, 🔶 including Marxist feminism as distinct from socialist. 🔶 Pateman or Okin where the question concerns citizenship, justice or the family. **Both** objection-reply chains — performativity versus category stability, and single-axis versus intersectionality. A cautious Indian application, correctly hedged. And a **graded** conclusion that names both the formal-equality achievement and the unfinished substantive agenda.

⚠️ **Directive decoding, as the owner states it.** **Discuss/Examine feminism as a critique of patriarchy** needs **both** the diagnosis and the feminist reply — ❌ do not describe patriarchy without the response. **Distinguish sex and gender** is definitional and analytical — give the distinction **plus why it matters**, not two one-line definitions. **Critically examine feminist perspectives on X** requires a thesis, at least two schools compared, at least one objection-reply, and a reasoned verdict — ❌ not a neutral list.

⚠️ **The quotation method, which governs the whole topic.** ⚠️ Prefer **paraphrased, named propositions over unverified verbatim quotations** — for example, Beauvoir's proposition that womanhood is socially produced rather than born — **unless the quotation is independently confirmed**. ⚠️ This credits the correct thinker and claim while avoiding misquotation risk, and it is why no sentence of Beauvoir, Butler or Crenshaw appears in quotation marks anywhere in this session.

### 5. The closing thesis, for any question in this topic

⚠️ **A single formulation that works as a conclusion across the topic**, drawn from the chapter's own final sentence and its two strongest findings: feminism's achievement was to win the argument about **rights** — **equal rights for women are no longer questioned in enlightened circles** — while its unfinished work is the distribution of **power**, because the chapter shows three separate goods being delivered without power following: legal standing at Lesson 16, health and education at Lesson 19's Finding 2, and prosperity at Finding 1. ⚠️ Hence the chapter's own demand for **the overall development of women, including improvement of their health and education as well as giving them adequate share in economic and political power** — both limbs, because neither implies the other.

Revision notes:
- ⚠️ Four discriminating questions: **where is the obstacle**, **who benefits**, **what counts as success**, 🔶 **which women**.
- ⚠️ Q2 is the cleanest separator: socialist feminism alone says **capitalists as well as men are the beneficiary**.
- ⚠️ Q4 is the chapter's structural silence — it differentiates women only by country.
- ⚠️ Four standing checks: 📰 **date** (1980 / late-20th-c / HDR 1995 / 2009; ❌ never **today**); 🔶 **provenance** (chapter / owner's extension / outside both); ❌ **boundary** (no article, section, case, amendment or current figure); ⚠️ **homogeneity** (never one undifferentiated class).
- ⚠️ 10 marks: thesis → sex/gender with the hinge → one stream → one limit → one-line conclusion.
- ⚠️ 15 marks: thesis → doctrine → two or three streams as claim/evidence/significance/**limit** → one objection-reply → one hedged Indian illustration → reasoned conclusion.
- ⚠️ 20 marks: thesis + directive decoding → full doctrine with Rousseau → all streams incl. 🔶 Marxist → 🔶 Pateman/Okin where relevant → **both** chains → hedged Indian application → **graded** conclusion.
- ⚠️ Directives: **Discuss/Examine** needs diagnosis **and** reply; **Distinguish** needs the distinction **plus why it matters**; **Critically examine** needs thesis + two schools + objection-reply + verdict.
- ⚠️ Quotation method: **paraphrased, named propositions over unverified verbatim quotations** unless independently confirmed.
- ⚠️ Closing thesis: the argument about **rights** was won; the distribution of **power** was not — three goods delivered without power following.

### Concept check

**Question:** You have ninety seconds before a 20-mark answer on feminist perspectives on equality. List the checks you run and what each prevents.

**Model answer:** First, **decode the directive** — **critically examine** requires a thesis, at least two schools compared, at least one objection-reply chain and a reasoned verdict, so the structure is fixed before any content is chosen; this prevents a neutral list. Second, run the **date check**: the 1980 United Nations figure, the late-twentieth-century legislature percentages, the **Human Development Report, 1995** and the Indian statutes called **recent** in a **2009** edition all need their period attached, and the sentence **the condition remains more or less unchanged today** must not be reproduced; this prevents the one error an examiner can establish with certainty. Third, run the **provenance check**: decide for each intended claim whether it sits in PDF pp.84-93, in the owner's extension — 🔶 Beauvoir, Oakley, Rubin, Marxist feminism, Pateman, Okin, Butler, Crenshaw — or outside both, and label accordingly; this prevents attributing a 1990 argument to a 2009 textbook. Fourth, run the **boundary check**: no constitutional article, section, amendment number, case citation or current statistic, since this topic owns the **conceptual** application only; this prevents an unverifiable legal assertion. Fifth, run the **homogeneity check**: scan for any sentence treating **women** as one undifferentiated class, and if one appears, apply Crenshaw's question with its limit that intersectionality **supplements, but should not replace**, the structural claims; this prevents the failure that is both analytical and reputational. ⚠️ Then write to the 20-mark shape — patriarchy, sex/gender, Rousseau's alterability, all streams including 🔶 Marxist, both objection-reply chains, one hedged Indian illustration, and a graded conclusion naming the rights achievement and the unfinished power agenda.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the four standing checks as presentational polish. They guard against precisely the four errors an examiner can identify with certainty and without argument — a dated statistic presented as current, a claim attributed to a source that does not contain it, a legal fact asserted without authority, and a generalisation about all women. Every other weakness in an answer is a matter of degree; these four are simply wrong, and they take under a minute to prevent.

# VERIFIED PYQ LINKAGE AND ANSWER APPROACHES

## The ownership position, stated plainly

This topic is supplementary conceptual support. No General Studies paper is printed as a Political Theory paper, and **no General Studies previous-year question is claimed here**. The basic owner maps this topic to **GS-II (Polity & Governance)** as a **conceptual foundation** and to **PSIR Optional Paper-1** as **background** — a mapping of concepts, not an ownership claim over any printed question.

⚠️ A bounded search pass in this session over reporting of the 2025 and 2026 Civil Services (Main) General Studies papers traced **no** question naming feminism, patriarchy or the sex/gender distinction as a political-theory doctrine. ⚠️ That is a search finding over reporting, not a line-by-line reading of the official texts, and it is recorded with that limit attached.

⚠️ ⚠️ **A second ownership layer applies here that did not apply to Topics 01-05, and it must be stated.** The anchors below are **verified local Philosophy Optional Paper II** stems, and every one of them is routed in the local ledgers to a **different primary owner**: the Philosophy **Gender Discrimination** file. ⚠️ Listing a stem here is therefore a claim about **what this topic contributes to an answer** — the sex/gender apparatus, the patriarchy analysis, the three schools, the representation argument — and **not** a claim that this topic owns the question. ➡️ The stems themselves belong to the Gender Discrimination owner, which the canonical owners name as the cross-syllabus support file for exactly this purpose.

No solved model answer appears below. Each entry gives the printed wording, the directive, the conceptual demand, the lesson that teaches what the answer needs, a concise approach, and a boundary.

⚠️ **Wording note applying to every exact stem in this section.** The stems are reproduced as transcribed in the local ledgers. A sibling topic in this subject established that the local 2018-2025 transcription sometimes closes up a printed space before a question mark; where the ledger preserves that space — as it does in the 2018 and 2026 stems below — it is preserved here, and where it does not, the ledger is followed and the uncertainty disclosed. Exact reproductions render typographic quotation marks and apostrophes as straight ASCII; that substitution alters no word and no other punctuation.

## Verified Philosophy Optional anchors — a separate application source

**2026, Paper II, Section A, Q2(b) [15 marks] (paper code KVMS-B-PHL)**
*"Does providing land and property rights to women constitute a necessary condition or a sufficient condition or both for ensuring social equality among the genders ? Discuss."*
- **Directive:** Discuss, but the real instruction is the **conditions apparatus** — necessary, sufficient, or both — and the answer must use it explicitly.
- **Apparatus tested:** whether a legal entitlement delivers social equality.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 8, 15, 16 and 19.
- **Approach:** this topic's contribution is the chapter's own repeated demonstration that an entitlement is not an outcome. **Necessary**: Lesson 8 names **the right to ownership of property** as one of three deprivations with **no logical connection with her biological status as a woman**, so its absence is a standing disability — and Lesson 15's **Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act, 2005**, **which gives equal right to daughters in joint family property**, is the chapter's own instance of the correction. **Not sufficient**: Lesson 16 establishes that **even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life**, and Lesson 19's Finding 2 generalises it — societies have **given adequate attention to health care and education of women, but no adequate share in the exercise of power**. ⚠️ Finding: necessary but not sufficient, with the chapter supplying the strongest available evidence for the second half.
- **Boundary:** ➡️ the land and property material, Indian legal detail and any empirical evidence on ownership belong to the Gender Discrimination owner and to Polity. ❌ No article, section or statistic from this topic.

**2025, Paper II, Section A, Q1(b) [10 marks] (SLPM-B-PHL)**
*How does gender as a social construct affect individuals' opportunities, rights, and access to resources? Critically discuss.*
- **Directive:** Critically discuss at 10 marks — a mechanism, named consequences, and one qualification.
- **Apparatus tested:** the construction claim and its three stated effects.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 3, 4, 7 and 8.
- **Approach:** the stem names exactly three outputs and this topic supplies a mechanism for each. **Opportunities**: Lesson 7's two curricula — **boys are encouraged to become doctors, engineers and lawyers; girls are encouraged to become school teachers, nurses or secretaries**. **Rights**: Lesson 8's three deprivations — **property**, **the vote**, **education and higher learning** — with **no logical connection with her biological status as a woman**. **Access to resources**: Lesson 6's 1980 ratio illustration, cited with its date. ⚠️ The critical element is the qualification: the chapter's claim is that these are **not directly based on biological differences**, and Lesson 4's three hedges should be reproduced rather than dropped.
- **Boundary:** ❌ No current data on any of the three outputs is asserted.

**2024, Paper II, Section A, Q4(a) [10+10=20 marks] (PHKM-B-PHL)**
*Discuss gender equality as a necessary condition to achieve empowerment of women. Also examine the role of women empowerment in curbing the menace of female foeticide.*
- **Directive:** two separately marked halves; the second is **examine**, so a finding is required.
- **Apparatus tested:** the equality/empowerment relation in the first half.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 15, 18 and 19 for the first half.
- **Approach:** ⚠️ this topic serves the **first half only**, and the entry says so. Its contribution is the chapter's own **protection / empowerment** sort at Lesson 15 and the **GDI / GEM** divergence at Lesson 19, which together show that equality of provision does not deliver empowerment — **adequate attention to health care and education of women, but no adequate share in the exercise of power**. ⚠️ That supports a graded answer to the first half: gender equality is necessary but, on the chapter's own evidence, not sufficient.
- **Boundary:** ❌ The second half — female foeticide — is wholly outside this topic. The chapter's only mention is the bare naming of the **Prenatal Diagnostic Techniques (Regulation and Prevention of Misuse) Act, 1994** at Lesson 15, with no analysis whatsoever. ➡️ Route the entire second half to the Gender Discrimination owner.

**2023, Paper II, Section A, Q3(c) [15 marks] (SKYC-B-PHLY)**
*How does gender discrimination lead to female foeticide and social imbalance? Discuss.*
- **Directive:** Discuss a causal chain.
- **Apparatus tested:** ⚠️ primarily the Gender Discrimination owner's; this topic supplies only the upstream concept.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 3 and 4 for the construction claim.
- **Approach:** ⚠️ **This topic's contribution is narrow and should be presented as narrow.** It can supply the first link only — that the devaluation is **cultural** rather than natural, since **gender inequalities are the product of convention and culture**, and that the valuation is installed through the mechanisms of Lessons 4 and 7. ❌ It supplies nothing about sex ratios, prenatal practice, regional variation or policy.
- **Boundary:** ➡️ the stem is routed to the **Gender Discrimination** owner and this topic contributes only the upstream claim that the devaluation is cultural. ❌ No sex-ratio figure, prenatal practice, regional pattern, policy or statute may be adduced from here; the chapter's sole mention of the subject is the bare naming of the **Prenatal Diagnostic Techniques (Regulation and Prevention of Misuse) Act, 1994** at Lesson 15, with no analysis attached. ➡️ Legal material to Polity.
- ⚠️ **Note:** 2023 also set **Q1(c) [10]** on whether **the rights concerning land and property have empowered women**, which this topic serves far better. ⚠️ That part sits in **Q1**, the 10-mark short-answer block, not in **Q3**, where this year's primary anchor sits. One anchor per year is listed; Q1(c) is named here so the curation is transparent.

**2022, Paper II, Section A, Q3(b) [15 marks] (CRNA-S-PHLY)**
*Discuss gender as a cultural category as opposed to sex as a biological category.*
- **Directive:** Discuss a distinction — the distinction **plus** why it matters, not two definitions.
- **Apparatus tested:** the core of this entire topic.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 3, 5 and 6, with Lesson 4 for the mechanism.
- **Approach:** ⚠️ **this is the stem this topic serves best of the nine**, and it should be answered from the chapter's own structure rather than from general knowledge. Open with the two definitions — sex **limited to biological differences such as reproductive function and secondary characteristics**, gender as **cultural ideas that construct images and expectations of both females and males** — and then the hinge, **nature has divided human race between men and women, but their status and role in society are determined by our culture**. Use the **'fair sex' / 'weaker sex' / 'ladies first'** test to show that an apparently biological phrase has **already entered the realm of culture**. Then supply the **point** of the distinction using Rousseau at Lesson 5: because gender inequalities are **the product of convention and culture**, they **can be questioned and removed wherever they are found objectionable**. Close with Lesson 6's reversal — **cultural factors have contributed to accentuate the biological differences** — which is the sophistication that separates a top answer. 🔶 Provenance from Lesson 20 may be added with its caveat.
- **Boundary:** ❌ Do not present the distinction as a denial that biological difference exists; the hinge sentence concedes it.

**2021, Paper II, Section A, Q4(b) [15 marks] (HXS-B-PHLY)**
*What are the main causes of female foeticide in India? Is it the result of demonic application of technology only? Discuss.*
- **Directive:** a causes question plus a reductionist thesis to be tested — **only** is the word to attack.
- **Apparatus tested:** ⚠️ primarily the Gender Discrimination owner's.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 4, 7 and 11 for the conceptual contribution.
- **Approach:** ⚠️ **this topic's contribution is a single structural point, and it is a strong one.** The chapter's own treatment of technology is **two-directional**: at Lesson 8 technology dissolved the justification for the division of labour while **the division of labour between men and women remained more or less unchanged**; at Lesson 11 Firestone treats technology as the **means of freeing themselves from the tyranny of their reproductive biology**; and at Lesson 9 technological advance is credited with having **given women the opportunity of proving their abilities**. ⚠️ So on this topic's own evidence technology is an **amplifier of prior valuations**, not an independent cause — which is precisely the answer to the **only** in the stem. ❌ All substantive content on the practice, its incidence and its regulation belongs elsewhere.
- **Boundary:** ➡️ the stem is routed to the **Gender Discrimination** owner and this topic contributes only the structural point about technology's two-directional role. ❌ No causal account of the practice, no incidence data and no regulatory or technological detail may be asserted from here. ⚠️ The chapter's own technology claims are themselves unevidenced — it records that advance **gave women the opportunity of proving their abilities** and that it left the division of labour **more or less unchanged**, without supporting either.
- ⚠️ **Note:** 2021 also set **Q1(e) [10]** on **land and property rights** and **empowerment of women**, which this topic serves better; ⚠️ that part sits in **Q1**, the 10-mark short-answer block, not in **Q4**, where this year's primary anchor sits, and it is named here for transparency.

**2020, Paper II, Section A, Q4(a) [20 marks] (URC-B-PHLY)**
*Do you agree that empowering women can eliminate gender discrimination? Discuss.*
- **Directive:** a position is required on a strong claim — **eliminate** is the word to test.
- **Apparatus tested:** the relation between empowerment and the structures that produce discrimination.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 15, 16, 18 and 19.
- **Approach:** attack the quantifier first. **Eliminate** is absolute, and the chapter supplies counter-evidence from three directions. ⚠️ Legal standing did not eliminate it — **even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life**. ⚠️ Development did not — **adequate attention to health care and education of women, but no adequate share in the exercise of power**. ⚠️ Prosperity did not — the report **does not find any correlation between gender bias and a country's economic development**. ⚠️ Then concede what is true: empowerment in the **GEM** sense — **the extent to which women participate in a country's economic and political life** — is exactly what the chapter says is missing, so the thesis is right about the **target** and wrong about the **sufficiency**. ⚠️ Close with the chapter's own both-limbs demand: **overall development … as well as … adequate share in economic and political power**.
- **Boundary:** ❌ No current programme, scheme or indicator is cited.

**2019, Paper II, Section A, Q3(b) [15 marks] (SDF-B-PHLY)**
*Consider critically that gender discrimination is a rather man-made concept but not naturally endowed.*
- **Directive:** consider critically — the proposition must be stated at full strength, tested, and a finding delivered.
- **Apparatus tested:** the natural/conventional distinction, and the limits of the construction claim.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 5, 6 and 8.
- **Approach:** ⚠️ this is the second-best fit of the nine and the chapter answers it almost directly. In support: Rousseau's distinction at Lesson 5, where conventional inequalities are **the product of our social arrangements** and therefore **alterable**, with the chart's own row — **Basis of Division: Product of Nature / Product of Culture**. In further support, Lesson 6's reversal, that **cultural factors have contributed to accentuate the biological differences**, which makes even part of the physical gap man-made. ⚠️ The critical element comes from the chapter's own concessions: Lesson 8 states that **biological differences between men and women necessitated the division of labour** at early stages, and Lesson 4 claims only that the traits are **not directly based on biological differences**. ⚠️ Finding: the proposition holds for the **hierarchy** and overstates if read as denying any biological input to the **origin** — which is exactly the position the chapter occupies.
- **Boundary:** ⚠️ the stem is routed in the ledgers to the **Gender Discrimination** owner, and this topic supplies the conceptual apparatus only — the natural/conventional distinction, the construction claim and the chapter's own concessions against it. ❌ No empirical evidence of discrimination, no sex-ratio or labour-market data, and no Indian statute, article or case may be adduced from here. ❌ The chapter's two supporting illustrations are unsourced, and **proved beyond doubt** is its own confidence, so neither may be cited as demonstrated fact. ➡️ Evidence of discrimination belongs to the Gender Discrimination owner; legal material to Polity.
- ⚠️ **Note:** 2019 also set **Q1(e) [10]**, *Can gender equality be realised within a socialist regime? Analyse.* — for which Lesson 13's socialist feminism and Lesson 19's quoted observation on **countries applying socialist models** are directly relevant, with the warning that the quoted passage supports a **GDI**-dimension claim only.

**2018, Paper II, Section A, Q4(c) [15 marks] (EGT-D-PHLY)**
*Is feminism an ideology for empowerment or for equality ? Discuss.*
- **Directive:** a forced choice to be resolved rather than accepted — and the resolution is the answer.
- **Apparatus tested:** the two definitions of feminism, and the equality/empowerment relation.
- **Taught in:** Lessons 1, 6, 15, 18 and 19.
- **Approach:** ⚠️ refuse the disjunction, and use the chapter's **own** two-part definition of feminist theory to do it: it implies **the advocacy of equal rights for women and men, accompanied by the commitment to improve the position of women in society** — equality **and** empowerment, in one sentence, with **accompanied by** making them inseparable. ⚠️ Then show why the question is nonetheless a good one: Lesson 15's **protection / empowerment** sort and Lesson 19's **GDI / GEM** divergence demonstrate that the two can come apart in practice, which is why a programme delivering only equality of provision fails. ⚠️ Close on the chapter's final sentence, which demands both limbs. ⚠️ A verdict that names the disjunction as false **and** explains why it feels forced — because the two goods are routinely delivered separately — is stronger than choosing a side.
- **Boundary:** ⚠️ the stem's spacing before the question mark is preserved as the ledger carries it.
- ⚠️ **Note:** 2018 also set **Q4(b) [15]** on gender discrimination **in the context of female foeticide**, which this topic serves only at the conceptual upstream; it is named here for transparency.

## Curation note

⚠️ **Nine year-entries across 2018-2026, supplying nine primary anchors — one per year, and no year is empty.** ⚠️ That is itself the finding: unlike Topics 01-05, where whole years passed without an owned stem, the Optional examiner has set a gender question in **every** year of the available record, and in several years more than one. ⚠️ Where a year offered a second candidate, the alternative is **named but not adopted**: **2023 Q1(c) [10]**, **2021 Q1(e) [10]**, **2019 Q1(e) [10]** and **2018 Q4(b) [15]**. ⚠️ So this section **references thirteen stems in all** — **nine primary anchors** set out in full with approaches, plus **four named secondary candidates** mentioned only for transparency of curation. ⚠️ The thirteen are not thirteen anchors; only the nine are.

⚠️ **A note on the Section A structure, since the numbering matters.** In these papers **Q1** is the block of five short parts at **10** marks each, while **Q2-Q4** carry the longer **15-** and **20-**mark parts. ⚠️ Three of the four secondary candidates — 2023's, 2021's and 2019's — sit in the **Q1** short-answer block and are therefore 10-markers; only 2018's secondary candidate, **Q4(b) [15]**, sits in a long-answer question. ⚠️ Each entry above states the question number and the mark value it actually carries.

⚠️ ⚠️ **All nine primary anchors are routed in the local ledgers to the Philosophy Gender Discrimination owner, not to this topic.**

⚠️ **The pattern across the nine primaries, computed from the nine entries alone and excluding the four secondary candidates.** ⚠️ These groups **overlap and are not a partition**, because two of the stems do two things at once; the overlaps are named rather than resolved.

| Demand | Primary anchors | Count |
|---|---|---|
| **Conceptual construction — the apparatus this topic owns** | **2025 Q1(b)** (gender as a social construct), **2022 Q3(b)** (gender as cultural category against sex as biological), **2019 Q3(b)** (man-made, not naturally endowed) | **3** |
| **Empowerment, and what it does or does not deliver** | **2024 Q4(a)** first half (gender equality as a necessary condition for empowerment), **2020 Q4(a)** (whether empowering women can eliminate discrimination), **2018 Q4(c)** (feminism as an ideology for empowerment or for equality) | **3** |
| **Female foeticide** | **2024 Q4(a)** second half, **2023 Q3(c)**, **2021 Q4(b)** | **3** |
| **Land and property rights** | **2026 Q2(b)** | **1** |

⚠️ **The overlaps, stated exactly.** **Three** of the nine primaries are dual in character — **2024 Q4(a)**, **2026 Q2(b)** and **2018 Q4(c)** — but only **one** of them, **2024 Q4(a)**, is **counted in two rows**; the other two are each counted **once**. That is why the column totals come to **ten** across nine distinct entries rather than to eleven or twelve.

⚠️ **Why each is dual, and where it was placed.** **2024 Q4(a)** is a two-part question whose halves have different subjects, so each printed half is counted in its own row. **2026 Q2(b)** is simultaneously a property stem and an equality/empowerment stem, since it asks whether property rights are a necessary or sufficient condition for **social equality among the genders**; it is counted **once**, under property, because that is its named subject. **2018 Q4(c)** also probes what feminism **is** as an ideology and so touches the conceptual group, but it is counted **once**, under empowerment, because the disjunction it sets — **empowerment or … equality** — is what the answer must resolve.

⚠️ **Two further observations the corrected numbering makes visible, with the counts stated rather than ranked.** First, across all **thirteen referenced stems** — the nine primaries plus the four secondaries — **female foeticide appears in four**: **2024 Q4(a)** (second half), **2023 Q3(c)**, **2021 Q4(b)** and the secondary **2018 Q4(b)**. **Land and property appears in three**: **2026 Q2(b)**, and the secondaries **2023 Q1(c)** and **2021 Q1(e)**. ⚠️ So land and property is a **recurrent** subject, appearing in three separate years, but it is **not** the most frequent; foeticide is set more often. ⚠️ What is distinctive about the land-and-property stems is their **placement rather than their frequency**: **two of the three sit in the Q1 short-answer block**, so the subject tends to be examined at **10** marks. Second, the conceptual group is the smallest in count yet carries the highest mark-weight concentration for this topic, since **2022 Q3(b)** and **2019 Q3(b)** are both **15**-markers answerable almost entirely from Lessons 3 to 6.

⚠️ The honest conclusion is that this topic is a **supplier** to the Gender Discrimination owner rather than a competitor with it: it provides the sex/gender distinction, the patriarchy analysis, the three schools and the representation argument that the other file's answers are built from, and it owns none of the stems. ⚠️ Only the **three** conceptual-construction primaries can be answered substantially from this topic alone; the other six require the owner's material for their substance.

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

### Cumulative check A — Lessons 1 to 5 (the apparatus)

**Question:** Build the chapter's analytical apparatus from the definition of feminism to the sex/gender chart, naming each step and what it adds.

**Model answer:** The chapter opens with a definition containing four separable claims: feminism concerns women's status **in relation to men**; they suffer injustice **because of their sex**; it **seeks effective measures for the redressal of that injustice**; and the inferior status is **the product of the institution of 'patriarchy'** and **not based on reason** — the fourth doing two jobs at once, making the arrangement alterable because institutional and unjustified because irrational (Lesson 1). Patriarchy is then boxed in two senses, the original being **a social system based on the authority of male head of the household** and the present **male domination in general, including its occurrence in labour market as well as domestic division of labour** — a widening that moves the term from family sociology into political theory. Early feminism is derived as an **extension** argument, emerging **in the wake of Enlightenment, which sought to enlarge the scope of 'rights of man' so as to include equal rights to women therein**, with Wollstonecraft and Taylor making **a fervent appeal** and Mill arguing that women were **by no means less talented** (Lesson 2). The analytical tool then arrives: sex is **limited to biological differences**, gender is **cultural ideas that construct images and expectations of both females and males**, and the hinge concedes and divides — **nature has divided human race between men and women, but their status and role in society are determined by our culture** (Lesson 3). Culture builds the hierarchy through paired trait-patterns which **serve as instruments of social control that reinforce male dominance**, enforced by re-description where authority in a woman **is termed to be indecent** (Lesson 4). Finally Rousseau supplies alterability: conventional inequalities are **the product of our social arrangements** and therefore **alterable**, so gender inequalities **can be questioned and removed wherever they are found objectionable** — set out in the chart's four rows from **Point of Division** to **Character of Division** (Lesson 5).

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not compress Rousseau's two-step licence into one. Conventionality makes an inequality **assessable** — it permits **a critical examination … from the point of view of justice** — and only a finding that it is **objectionable** makes it removable. "Conventional, therefore unjust" is invalid and weaker than the chapter's actual claim, which is that convention strips an arrangement of the immunity naturalness confers.

### Cumulative check B — Lessons 6 to 9 (persistence and the rights agenda)

**Question:** How does the chapter explain that an arrangement with a functional origin became an injustice, and what does it deprive itself of in doing so?

**Model answer:** By locating the injustice in **persistence** rather than in origin, which costs it a large and deliberate concession. It concedes that at **early stages of social organization, biological differences between men and women necessitated the division of labour between them**, and that the resulting system was **based on mutual care and adjustment, and did not involve any significant level of resentment**. ⚠️ The hinge then does the work: **with the development of technology, sweeping changes took place in other parts of social organization, but the division of labour between men and women remained more or less unchanged** — a justification expired while the arrangement survived, which is the definition of a convention under Lesson 5's test. ⚠️ The divergence of power follows, with man **acquiring more and more power** while woman **continued to hold the subordinate position**, and the gap is filled with compensation rather than redistribution — **concessions and exemptions**, protection, **shyness … eulogized as a woman's ornament**, **'fair sex'**, **'ladies first'**, chivalry — all honorific, none costing the dominant party anything, several reinforcing what they appear to soften. ⚠️ The verdict is that she **was given security but not an opportunity to learn certain things that would make her as competent as man, and thereby vindicate her claim to equality**, with three deprivations named — **property**, **the vote**, **education and higher learning** — all having **no logical connection with her biological status as a woman**. ⚠️ What the chapter deprives itself of is any claim that the arrangement was always and only domination; what it gains is a non-sentimental answer to its own question of why the injustice surfaced recently, and the Lesson 6 reinforcement that **cultural factors have contributed to accentuate the biological differences**, which makes the biological evidence itself partly an artefact of the arrangement.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not repeat **the condition remains more or less unchanged today** or the 1980 figures as current. The report is from **1980**, the **today** belongs to a **2009** edition, and the chapter offers no evidence for the continuity claim. Cite the **shape** of the ratios — a share falling steeply from work performed to income received to property owned — with the year attached.

### Cumulative check C — Lessons 10 to 13 (the three schools)

**Question:** Separate the three schools the chapter names, using the questions that actually discriminate between them.

**Model answer:** By where each locates the obstacle, who it says benefits, and what would count as success. **Liberal feminism** aims at **the revival of the conventional feminist movement** and locates the obstacle in formal barriers, insisting on **absolute equality of opportunity for men and women in all walks of life and complete removal of gender-based discrimination**, with a programme of **equal pay for equal work, abortion laws reform** and **increasing representation**; the chapter names no thinker for it and judges it **the most popular stream … but it is not considered to be very influential**. **Radical feminism** locates the obstacle at the root, and has two pillars with a division of labour: Firestone answers **why**, holding that subordination **could not be understood as a symptom or aspect of some deeper or more comprehensive system of domination**, that its basis **was ultimately biological** through the chain in which **infants should depend on lactating women and women in turn, should depend on men**, and that **reliable contraceptives, baby foods and 'test-tube babies'** now supply the exit; Millett answers **what**, holding the relation **based on power and further sustained by an ideology**, **similar to** class and race and therefore to be **treated as political relationship**, with domination exercised **through social authority and economic force**. **Socialist feminism** is a **combination of patriarchal analysis of radical feminism and class analysis of Marxism**, and its decisive claim is the double beneficiary — **capitalists as well as men are the beneficiary of women's subordination** — with Rowbotham's **participatory, decentralized approach** and the finding that success comes **only through combining these struggles**. ⚠️ The cleanest single discriminator is the beneficiary question: one beneficiary makes a school radical or reductive, two make it dual-system.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not read Firestone and Millett as contradicting each other. Firestone denies that gender oppression is **derived from** class or race; Millett asserts that it **resembles** them. Derivation and resemblance are different relations, and the resemblance is what licenses Millett to import **Max Weber's theory of domination** without importing any causal claim.

### Cumulative check D — Lessons 14 to 19 (after rights)

**Question:** The chapter shows three different goods being delivered without power following. Name them and explain what the sequence establishes.

**Model answer:** First, **legal standing**. Lesson 16 records the defeated assumption that after legal equality **women were not left with any issue of complaint**, and replaces it with the thesis that **even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life** — demonstrated by the residue that survived the turnout convergence, since **more and more women were taking part in voting, but their share at various levels of political authority had remained insignificant**. Second, **health and education**. Lesson 19's Finding 2 records that **there is no essential correlation between GDI and GEM in many cases**, meaning societies have **given adequate attention to health care and education of women, but no adequate share in the exercise of power**, illustrated by the **'economic tigers' of East Asia**. Third, **prosperity**. Finding 1 records that the report **does not find any correlation between gender bias and a country's economic development**, with Cuba ranking **72 on Human Development Index** but **16 on the GEM**. ⚠️ What the sequence establishes is that power is not a by-product of anything else — not of rights, not of welfare, not of wealth — and must be targeted directly. ⚠️ That is why the chapter's closing sentence demands both limbs, **the overall development of women, including improvement of their health and education as well as giving them adequate share in economic and political power**, and why Lesson 18's second view turns to **reservations** as a distinct instrument. ⚠️ Lesson 17's Scandinavian exception completes the argument by showing the gap is contingent: **twenty-six per cent** in Sweden and Denmark and **thirty-four per cent** in Norway against **less than five per cent** in the British House of Commons proves a variable that moves across societies, and therefore a conventional and alterable one.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not cite any figure in this block as current. The legislature percentages are late-twentieth-century, the indices are from the **Human Development Report, 1995**, the Charter of Paris is **November 1990** and the heads-of-state list ends in **2007** — all inside a **2009** edition. What survives dating is the structure of the findings, not the values.

### Cumulative check E — 🔶 Lessons 20 to 23 (beyond the chapter)

**Question:** What do the owner's extension sections supply that the chapter cannot, and what discipline governs their use?

**Model answer:** They supply four things the chapter lacks, each with a stated limit. 🔶 **Provenance**: the chapter states the sex/gender distinction without attribution, and the owner supplies Beauvoir's proposition that **womanhood is a social becoming rather than a fixed biological given** as the root, Oakley as **usually credited with popularising** the terminology, and Rubin's **sex/gender system** as the mechanism that **explains how sex becomes gender, which Gauba's chapter asserts but does not mechanise** — with the mandatory caveat against presenting any **single, uncontested "origin story" as settled fact**. 🔶 **A fourth school**: Marxist feminism, holding that oppression is generated **principally by the capitalist mode of production, especially unpaid domestic/reproductive labour**, evidenced by the **1970s "domestic labour debate"**, which supplies the content of Gauba's otherwise empty phrase about **analysis of labour, both wage-labour and domestic labour**. 🔶 **Critiques of the liberal tradition**: Pateman's claim that contract theory assumes a **prior "sexual contract"** — an **interpretive lens, not a documented historical event** — and Okin's that justice theory wrongly treats **the family as a pre-political given**, both reaching places the chapter documents but does not theorise. 🔶 **The live debates**: Butler's performativity with its three use-conditions, and Crenshaw's intersectionality, which **forces liberal/radical/socialist/Marxist feminism to ask which women a given claim describes, correcting an unstated universalism** while **supplementing, not replacing** them. ⚠️ The discipline is a three-tier provenance rule: Tier 1 is PDF pp.84-93 and is cited by page; Tier 2 is this extension material, marked 🔶 and never cited to Gauba; Tier 3 is outside both, and the only Tier-3 names used are **Engels** and **Friedan**, whose absence from the sources is declared rather than filled.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not let intersectionality dissolve the radical-feminist claim it corrects. The owner is explicit that the reply **strengthens rather than replaces the original radical-feminist insight that sexual/reproductive domination is analytically distinct from class or race domination**. Asking **which** women a claim describes corrects its scope; it does not show that gendered domination is not a distinct kind.

### Cumulative check F — the whole topic

**Question:** You have two minutes before a 20-mark answer on feminism and equality. List the checks you run, in order, and what each prevents.

**Model answer:** First, **decode the directive** and fix the structure before choosing content — **critically examine** needs thesis, two schools, an objection-reply chain and a verdict; **distinguish** needs the distinction plus why it matters; this prevents a neutral list. Second, run the **date check**: the United Nations figure is **1980**, the legislature percentages and the Charter of Paris are late twentieth century, the indices are **1995**, the Indian statutes are called **recent** in a **2009** edition, and the sentence **the condition remains more or less unchanged today** may never be reproduced; this prevents the one error an examiner can establish without argument. Third, run the **provenance check** across the three tiers — chapter, 🔶 owner's extension, outside both — and label each claim; this prevents attributing a 1990 argument to a 2009 textbook. Fourth, run the **boundary check**: no constitutional article, section, amendment number, case citation or current statistic, since this topic owns the **conceptual** application only and routes law to Polity; this prevents an unverifiable legal assertion. Fifth, run the **homogeneity check**: scan for any sentence treating **women** as one undifferentiated class, and apply Crenshaw's question with its limit; this prevents the failure that is simultaneously analytical and reputational. Sixth, select **evidence units with their limitations attached** — the hinge sentence, the trait-pairs with their three hedges, Rousseau's two-step licence, the 1980 ratios with their year, Firestone's chain, Millett's two forms, Rowbotham's double beneficiary, the Scandinavian exception, the GDI/GEM divergence. Seventh, choose an **objection-reply chain** and concede its residual. Then write, closing on the graded verdict: the argument about rights was won, the distribution of power was not.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the standing checks as polish applied after writing. Each guards against an error that is simply wrong rather than merely weak — a dated statistic presented as current, a claim attributed to a source that does not contain it, a legal fact asserted without authority, and a generalisation about all women — and all four take under a minute to prevent.

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

⚠️ The questions below are original practice prompts written for this session. They are not previous-year questions and must not be cited as such.

### 10-mark practice (~150 words)

**Practice question 1 [10]:** Distinguish sex from gender, and explain why the distinction matters politically. *(Distinguish — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer:** Sex is limited to biological differences such as reproductive function and secondary characteristics; gender refers to cultural ideas that construct images and expectations of both females and males. The distinction concedes the first and limits its reach: nature has divided the human race between men and women, but their status and role in society are determined by our culture. ⚠️ Its political significance lies in what follows for alterability. Applying Rousseau's division, the sexual division is natural and largely unalterable, while gender inequalities are the product of convention and culture, so they can be questioned and removed wherever they are found objectionable. The distinction therefore converts an apparently fixed hierarchy into an arrangement answerable to justice. ⚠️ Its sharpest use is diagnostic: phrases like fair sex, weaker sex and ladies first look biological but have already entered the realm of culture.

**Scoring note for this answer:** a Distinguish directive is failed by two definitions, so the marks sit in the second half. The decisive move is deriving **alterability** from the distinction rather than asserting it, and the closing diagnostic test shows the distinction being **used** rather than merely stated.

**Practice question 2 [10]:** Why does the chapter hold that biological differences cannot justify women's inferior status? *(Why — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer:** Because the justification is circular and the evidence it relies on is partly manufactured by the arrangement it defends. The chapter's claim is not merely that biology is irrelevant to status but that cultural factors have contributed to accentuate the biological differences between men and women. ⚠️ Two kinds of evidence are offered. Diachronically, the rapid improvement in women's athletic records is taken as an indication that social norms had shaped biology and restricted women's physical development. Synchronically, feminist anthropologists are reported as finding that in some ethnic groups physical differentiation between men and women is not as prominent as in others. ⚠️ Together these cover the two ways a supposed constant can be disproved — variation across time and across places. The conclusion is hedged: the present status is chiefly the product of social arrangements. ⚠️ Neither illustration is sourced, and proved beyond doubt is the author's confidence.

**Scoring note for this answer:** a Why question is failed by description, so the answer gives the **structure** of the argument — circularity — rather than its conclusion. Naming the two kinds of evidence as diachronic and synchronic, and then attaching the honest limitation, is what converts a reproduced paragraph into an assessed one.

### 15-mark practice (~200-250 words)

**Practice question 3 [15]:** Examine the claim that formal legal equality is sufficient to end women's subordination. *(Examine — answer in 200 to 250 words.)*

**Model answer:** Restated as testable, the claim is that removing discriminatory law removes the subordination it sustained. It was once widely held: earlier it was usually thought that after establishing legal equality of men and women, women were not left with any issue of complaint. ⚠️ It also follows validly from a purely formal conception of equality, so it is not a foolish position but an incomplete standard.

The counter-evidence is decisive and comes from three directions. First, legal standing: even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life — and while turnout converged, their share at various levels of political authority had remained insignificant. Second, provision: there is no essential correlation between GDI and GEM in many cases, meaning societies gave adequate attention to health care and education of women, but no adequate share in the exercise of power. Third, prosperity: the 1995 report does not find any correlation between gender bias and a country's economic development.

Attacking the logic: law reaches rules, whereas the chapter's mechanism runs through belief, since women are made to believe that the proper sphere of their activity is within their household — and a belief survives the repeal of every rule.

⚠️ Finding: the claim fails. Formal equality is necessary and not sufficient, which is why the remedy proposed is cultural transformation of society alongside compensatory measures.

**Scoring note for this answer:** "Examine" requires the claim stated at full strength, evidence both ways and a finding. The distinguishing move is conceding that the claim follows validly from a formal conception of equality before defeating it — and the logical attack, that law reaches rules while the mechanism runs through belief, explains **why** the evidence came out as it did.

**Practice question 4 [15]:** "Socialist feminism merely adds class to radical feminism." Critically examine. *(Critically examine — answer in 200 to 250 words.)*

**Model answer:** The proposition has a surface warrant, since socialist feminism is introduced as a combination of patriarchal analysis of radical feminism and class analysis of Marxism — an addition in the chapter's own words.

It nonetheless fails, on three grounds. First, the combination generates a claim neither parent makes: that capitalists as well as men are the beneficiary of women's subordination. Two beneficiaries entail two systems, which blocks reduction in both directions — against orthodox Marxism and against the strongest radical claim alike. Second, it produces a distinctive analytical programme, the analysis of labour, both wage-labour and domestic labour, which brings unpaid household work inside political economy; neither parent had done so. Third, it changes the method: Rowbotham advocates a participatory, decentralized approach contemplating linking of the struggles of all oppressed groups, where Firestone ranks oppressions by treating women as the first oppressed group.

Attacking the logic: "merely adds" assumes combination is additive, but a synthesis that yields propositions absent from both inputs is not.

⚠️ Verdict: the proposition fails as stated. ⚠️ The honest residual is that the chapter itself tilts, describing Rowbotham as a Marxist for whom women's liberation is essentially bound with the struggle against capitalism — which is exactly the risk of submerging gendered experience inside anti-capitalist politics.

**Scoring note for this answer:** the critical element is discharged by attacking the inference — that combination must be additive — rather than only the conclusion, and then conceding a residual the chapter genuinely leaves open. Naming the tilt in the Rowbotham paragraph shows the source has been read against itself rather than summarised.

### 20-mark practice (~250-300 words)

**Practice question 5 [20]:** Critically evaluate the claim that equal treatment under the same laws is the proper standard of gender equality. *(Critically evaluate — answer in 250 to 300 words.)*

**Model answer:** Evaluation needs a criterion. The one adopted here is whether a standard, applied faithfully, would remove the subordination it addresses.

The case for sameness is real. One view holds that there is no difference between men and women as regards their capabilities, hence they should be governed by the same laws — and the whole rights tradition rests on it, since Wollstonecraft's claim was that women, like men, are rational individuals. It also carries a protection: identical rules cannot be used to narrow opportunity, which is what the nineteenth-century campaigns for removing educational and professional barriers fought against.

Against it, the criterion is not met. Subordination is produced by arrangements rather than rules — women are made to believe that the proper sphere of their activity is within their household — and identical rules leave the arrangements standing. The chapter's evidence is that full citizenship in law left subjection in their social life untouched, while electorates of nearly fifty per cent returned legislatures of less than ten per cent.

The competing view holds that women are essentially different from men — biologically, culturally and socially — and should have equal opportunities to develop and apply their distinctive capabilities along with equal rights, supporting maternity provision and reservations.

⚠️ But its measures are not uniform. Provision supports a burden only one party bears; reservations correct a documented under-representation; exemption from hazardous tasks actually narrows opportunity, which is the structure patriarchy used in granting concessions to the weaker sex.

⚠️ Graded verdict: sameness fails the criterion; difference meets it only where the measure enlarges rather than restricts standing — which is the discriminator the chapter endorses without stating.

**Scoring note for this answer:** "Critically evaluate" is failed by an unnamed criterion, so it is declared first and every judgment refers back to it. The lift comes from refusing to treat the second view as a single position — separating provision, compensation and exemption — and then supplying the discriminator (enlarges versus restricts) that the chapter endorses view (b) without ever articulating.

**Practice question 6 [20]:** "Feminism cannot speak of women as a single category." Discuss. *(Discuss — answer in 250 to 300 words.)*

**Model answer:** The proposition must be stated at full strength before it is tested, because it is the sharpest objection the field faces.

Its warrant is strong. 🔶 Treating race and sex discrimination as separate, additive categories fails women who face a distinct, compounded disadvantage at their intersection — the illustration being claims falling through the gap between a race-discrimination template and a sex-discrimination template, each implicitly modelled on a different unmarked group. The target is unstated universalism, and the source chapter is squarely exposed: it speaks of women throughout and differentiates them only by country, when its own evidence shows legislative shares ranging from under five per cent to thirty-four per cent. 🔶 A second pressure comes from performativity, on which gender is produced through repeated, stylised acts, so that even the category's stability is in question.

Against the proposition, two replies. 🔶 First, political claims can be built on provisionally, strategically stabilised categories, and legal sex-discrimination categories function pragmatically regardless of deeper philosophical instability. Second, and more decisively, the objection concerns scope rather than existence: showing that domination is compounded and differently configured across axes does not show that sexual and reproductive domination is not analytically distinct from class or race domination.

Attacking the logic: the inference runs from internal variation to categorial unusability, which does not follow — classes and nations vary internally and remain analysable.

⚠️ Verdict: the proposition succeeds as a correction and fails as a prohibition. 🔶 Intersectionality supplements, but should not replace, the structural claims of the schools; the defensible position is that patriarchy is one structure operating differently on differently situated women.

**Scoring note for this answer:** a Discuss stem requires both sides fairly stated and a verdict, and the distinguishing move is the logical attack — naming the inference from internal variation to unusability as a non-sequitur. Conceding that the source chapter is itself exposed to the objection, rather than defending it, is what makes the eventual verdict credible.

# REMEDIATION

Each entry names an error, the correction, and where the correction is taught.

| # | Error | Correction | Taught in |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Treating feminism as a subject about women rather than a claim about a relation | It concerns **the status and role of women in society in relation to men** — relational, which is why it belongs in political theory | L1 |
| 2 | Reading "injustice because of their sex" as "sex causes the injustice" | Sex is the **marker** by which victims are selected; the **source** named is **the institution of 'patriarchy'** | L1 |
| 3 | Defining patriarchy as household authority only | That is the **original** sense; it is **now** applied to **male domination in general, including its occurrence in labour market as well as domestic division of labour** | L1 |
| 4 | Treating early feminism as a new principle | It was an **extension** argument — the Enlightenment **sought to enlarge the scope of 'rights of man' so as to include equal rights to women therein** | L2 |
| 5 | Crediting Wollstonecraft with Mill's argument, or the reverse | She **argued** women are **rational individuals** and **established the principles**; he **sought to demonstrate** they were **in no way inferior … in their talents** and **pleaded** | L2, L9 |
| 6 | Attributing a thesis to Harriet Taylor from this chapter, or reproducing her dates uncritically | ❌ She is named with dates and placed with Wollstonecraft; **no** work, argument or further mention is given. ⚠️ The chapter prints **(1807-59)**; the standard form is **(1807-1858)** — use the standard in your own voice | L2 |
| 7 | Defining gender as "social roles" | It is **cultural ideas that construct images and expectations** — the constructing machinery, of which roles are the output | L3 |
| 8 | Treating gender as a synonym for women | The definition covers **both females and males**; masculinity is as much a construction as femininity | L3, L4 |
| 9 | Reading the sex/gender distinction as denying biological difference | The hinge concedes it: **nature has divided human race between men and women, but their status and role in society are determined by our culture** | L3 |
| 10 | Missing the "intrinsic worth" clause | Women are **placed in a lower status in relation to their intrinsic worth** — ⚠️ the standard that makes "lower" evaluative rather than merely comparative | L3 |
| 11 | Treating 'fair sex' and 'ladies first' as harmless courtesies | **Our attention is not confined to the biological fact, but have already entered the realm of culture** — ⚠️ and Lesson 8 shows them functioning as compensation in place of power | L3, L8 |
| 12 | Listing masculine and feminine traits as eight separate items | They are **two opposite patterns** in four pairs; **logical outlook / intuitiveness** places women on the wrong side of the rationality criterion, and **dominance / submissiveness** is the only overtly hierarchical pair | L4 |
| 13 | Stripping the hedges from the near-universality claim | Keep all three: **relative**, **almost universal**, **not directly based on biological differences** | L4 |
| 14 | Presenting the three named causes as the chapter's derivation of the three schools | ❌ The chapter lists causes at p.85 and schools at pp.88-90 and never connects them; the mapping is a reading | L4, L10 |
| 15 | Missing how the control is enforced | By **re-description**, not prohibition: a woman behaving authoritatively **is termed to be indecent** | L4 |
| 16 | Collapsing Rousseau's two-step licence | Conventional → **assessable** (**a critical examination … from the point of view of justice**); **found objectionable** → removable. Convention removes immunity, it does not convict | L5 |
| 17 | Treating natural inequality as permanently fixed | The chapter writes **largely unalterable**, and supplies two counter-pressures of its own — athletic records, and Firestone's technological exit | L5, L6, L11 |
| 18 | Quoting the 1980 ratios as a current statistic | ❌ They are from a **United Nations Report, 1980** inside a **2009** book; cite the **shape** of the ratios with the year attached | L6 |
| 19 | Repeating "the condition remains more or less unchanged today" | ❌ Never. The **today** is the 2009 edition's, and the chapter supplies **no** evidence for the continuity claim | L6 |
| 20 | Stating only that biology is irrelevant to status | The stronger claim is the reversal — **cultural factors have contributed to accentuate the biological differences** — which makes the justification **circular** | L6 |
| 21 | Treating "proved beyond doubt" as a scientific consensus | ⚠️ It is the chapter's own confidence; the two illustrations that follow are unsourced and undated | L6 |
| 22 | Describing the exclusion from public life as a prohibition | Women **are made to believe that the proper sphere of their activity is within their household** — ⚠️ internal, needing no enforcement, surviving the repeal of every rule | L7 |
| 23 | Reading the occupational lists as skilled versus unskilled | **School teachers** and **nurses** are trained professions; the discriminator is **autonomy and command**, which is why the experience does not transfer to **a political career** | L7 |
| 24 | Hiding the chapter's concession on the origin of the division of labour | ⚠️ State it: **biological differences … necessitated the division of labour**, with **no significant level of resentment**. The concession is what locates the injustice in **persistence** | L8 |
| 25 | Missing the hinge of the historical account | **With the development of technology, sweeping changes took place in other parts of social organization, but the division of labour between men and women remained more or less unchanged** | L8 |
| 26 | Presenting the Stage 4 concessions as partial compensation | ⚠️ All are honorific and none redistributive; protection **restricts**, eulogised shyness entrenches, chivalry requires distress. The verdict is **systematically deprived of her share in power** | L8 |
