| 27 | Forgetting the fifth 1792 disability | **No real right to divorce even if her husband abused her** — ⚠️ an exclusion from **exit** from a private relation, not from a public good. Keep **no real right** | L9 |
| 28 | Supplying suffragette organisations or episodes | ❌ The chapter says only that the spirit was **often quite militant** and **culminated in early twentieth century struggles for women's suffrage**; no movement, country or date is named | L9 |
| 29 | Quoting "equal rights … no longer questioned" without its restriction | Keep **in enlightened circles**, **most of the jobs that men do**, and the **2009** vantage — the claim is about the argumentative terrain only | L9 |
| 30 | Treating the three-school typology as exhaustive | The chapter's own sentence: feminist theory evolved into **various schools**, of which **three are particularly important** | L10 |
| 31 | Naming Wollstonecraft or Mill as the chapter's liberal feminists | ❌ The chapter names **no** thinker for liberal feminism. They may be used as the **conventional feminist movement** it **revives**, with that stated | L10 |
| 32 | Treating liberal feminism's programme as derivable from its principle | ⚠️ **Abortion laws reform** concerns a capacity only one sex has, and **increasing representation** names an outcome; neither follows from **absolute equality of opportunity** | L10, L18 |
| 33 | Reading "radical" as extreme | It means **root-level** — subordination **could not be understood as a symptom or aspect of some deeper or more comprehensive system of domination** | L11 |
| 34 | Presenting Firestone as making biology destiny | ❌ She names the exit: **reliable contraceptives, baby foods and 'test-tube babies'** supply **the means of freeing themselves from the tyranny of their reproductive biology**; **hitherto** marks the end of the inevitability | L11 |
| 35 | Treating the dependence chain as uniformly biological | **Infants should depend on lactating women** is biological; **women in turn, should depend on men** is a social arrangement conditional on material scarcity — which is why technology can break it | L11 |
| 36 | Reading the technological exit as abolishing care | The aim is **diffusing the child-bearing and child-rearing role to society as a whole, men as well as women** — redistribution, not abolition | L11, L14 |
| 37 | Treating Firestone's chronological priority as an argument for causal independence | ⚠️ **Historically women constituted the first oppressed group** establishes order, not present cause; state the gap | L11 |
| 38 | Reading Millett's analogy as contradicting Firestone | **Similar to** is not **reducible to**; resemblance licenses borrowing **Max Weber's theory of domination** without importing a causal claim | L12 |
| 39 | Expanding "Weber's theory of domination" into his typology | ❌ The chapter names the theory and nothing else; its own content is **social authority** and **economic force**. ➡️ Power and authority owner | L12 |
| 40 | Attributing the slogan "the personal is political" to the chapter | ❌ Not on the page. What is there is the argument that the relation **should be treated as political relationship** | L12 |
| 41 | Presenting socialist feminism as reducing gender to class | ❌ Socialist feminists **have also considered the role of culture and psycho-analytical aspects of sexuality** | L13 |
| 42 | Missing the decisive socialist-feminist claim | **Capitalists as well as men are the beneficiary of women's subordination** — two beneficiaries, two systems, a dual-system position | L13 |
| 43 | Treating Rowbotham as ranking oppressions | She advocates **linking of the struggles of all oppressed groups** — ⚠️ a real disagreement with Firestone, and the seed of 🔶 Chain 2 | L13, 🔶 L22 |
| 44 | Smoothing over the Rowbotham tilt | ⚠️ The symmetric opening sits awkwardly with **as a Marxist** and **essentially bound with the struggle against capitalism**; the chapter does not resolve it | L13 |
| 45 | Dismissing the non-sexist vocabulary demand as trivial | ⚠️ The chapter's own mechanism runs through language — **made to believe**, **termed to be indecent** — so vocabulary is a working part, not decoration | L14 |
| 46 | Reading the movement's agenda as primarily legislative | Its demand was that **myths, values and beliefs … should be fundamentally reassessed and changed**; outcomes were **new laws in some countries** but **worldwide acceptance of new norms** | L14 |
| 47 | Using the Indian statute list as a current legal account | ❌ Short titles and years only, **recent** as of **2009**; no amendment, repeal, judgment or post-2009 statute is carried. ➡️ Polity | L15 |
| 48 | Reporting the panchayat projections as achieved | ❌ **Can be increased to one-half** and provision for **legislative assemblies and parliament** are **2009 expectations**; **a beginning** is the chapter's own word for what exists | L15 |
| 49 | Missing the protection/empowerment sort | ⚠️ It is the chapter's own and maps directly onto Lesson 8's **security but not an opportunity** | L8, L15 |
| 50 | Treating the defeated "no issue of complaint" assumption as a foolish prejudice | ⚠️ It follows validly from a **formal** conception of equality; its collapse shows that standard to be incomplete, which is the premise of the rest of the chapter | L16 |
| 51 | Missing where the problem relocated | Turnout converged but **their share at various levels of political authority had remained insignificant** — the explanandum was never participation | L16 |
| 52 | Quoting the representation percentages as current | ❌ Late-twentieth-century illustrations in a **2009** book; cite with the period attached, and note the chapter gives **no** US figure, only **not very different** | L17 |
| 53 | Skipping the Scandinavian exception | ⚠️ It is the decisive logical point — **twenty-six per cent** and **thirty-four per cent** against **less than five per cent** show the gap is **contingent**, hence conventional and alterable. ❌ The chapter explains **why** nowhere | L17 |
| 54 | Using the list of women heads of state to soften the conclusion | The chapter's own discriminator: such records are **enough proof of the potential of women's power. But it is no proof of the opportunities open to women** | L17 |
| 55 | Reading view (b) as the essentialism the chapter attacks | Its difference is **culturally and socially** constituted too, and **equal rights** are retained; it argues about what equal treatment **requires** | L18 |
| 56 | Treating the four kinds of measure as one demand | **Provision** supports a burden one party bears; **compensation** corrects documented **under-representation**; **exemption** from **underground mining and working in night shifts** actually **narrows** opportunity | L18 |
| 57 | Citing the chapter's endorsement as an argument | ⚠️ **This view seems to be more reasonable and is widely endorsed** is hedged and supported by **no** reasoning in the chapter | L18 |
| 58 | Confusing GDI with GEM | GDI tracks **health care and education**; GEM is **the extent to which women participate in a country's economic and political life** — provision versus power | L19 |
| 59 | Reading the socialist-models quotation as a claim about power | ⚠️ It credits **rapid and equal progress in education and health** — a **GDI** claim; Finding 2 warns that GDI gains need not bring GEM gains | L19 |
| 60 | Generalising from the "most gender-biased societies" characterisation | ❌ Reproduce only as a dated report of a dated index; never as a causal or regional claim — and the chapter's own Finding 1 undercuts a simple reading | L19 |
| 61 | 🔶 Presenting the sex/gender distinction as having a single originator | ❌ **Do not present a single, uncontested "origin story" as settled fact — the distinction has multiple co-formulators and was consolidated gradually; avoid taxonomic overclaim** | 🔶 L20 |
| 62 | 🔶 Quoting Beauvoir verbatim | ❌ Paraphrase only — **womanhood is a social becoming rather than a fixed biological given**; the owner's method prefers **paraphrased, named propositions over unverified verbatim quotations** | 🔶 L20, L24 |
| 63 | 🔶 Treating Marxist and socialist feminism as synonyms | **Marxist feminism** → lead with the domestic-labour/social-reproduction claim; **socialist feminism** → lead with Rowbotham's dual systems; **the two labels overlap but are not simple synonyms** | 🔶 L21 |
| 64 | 🔶 Presenting Pateman's sexual contract as a historical event | ❌ It is **an interpretive lens, not a documented historical event**, and must not be used as a factual claim about any contract being signed | 🔶 L21 |
| 65 | 🔶 Reconstructing Rawls from Okin's critique | ❌ The owner names **primary goods** and nothing more. ➡️ Justice owner | 🔶 L21 |
| 66 | 🔶 Reporting Butler as denying that bodies exist | ❌ The claim is about how **the meaning and categorisation of bodies is produced**; and it must never be presented as Gauba's position | 🔶 L22 |
| 67 | 🔶 Using intersectionality to dismiss radical feminism | ⚠️ It **strengthens rather than replaces** the insight that **sexual/reproductive domination is analytically distinct from class or race domination**, and **supplements, but should not replace**, the schools' structural claims | 🔶 L22 |
| 68 | 🔶 Attaching dates or numbers to Savitribai Phule | ❌ **Do not attach specific unverified school-opening dates or numbers**; the ceiling is **early advocate of women's education against caste-and-gender exclusion** | 🔶 L23 |
| 69 | 🔶 Citing bill provisions or sections for Ambedkar | ❌ Treat as **a general, well-known orientation of his thought**; exact legislative detail is outside the verified source base. ➡️ Polity | 🔶 L23 |
| 70 | Supplying Engels or Friedan as this topic's material | ❌ Neither is named in PDF pp.84-93 or in either owner. ⚠️ *The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State* (**1884**) and *The Feminine Mystique* (**1963**) are verified titles and years; the absence must be declared and no claim attributed | 🔶 L23 |
| 71 | Writing "women" as a single undifferentiated class | ⚠️ Apply the **which women** check; the chapter differentiates women only by country | 🔶 L22, L24 |
| 72 | Reproducing the chapter's name forms in your own voice | ⚠️ Chapter prints **Rowbatham** (standard **Rowbotham**), **Millet** alongside **Millett**, and **Berkley** (standard **Berkeley**) | L12, L13, L14 |
| 73 | Dating *Sexual Politics* to 1971 in your own voice | ⚠️ The chapter prints **(1971)**; the verified standard first publication is **1970**. Reproduce the chapter's date only when quoting it | L12 |
| 74 | Using the chapter's open life-dates | ⚠️ **Firestone (1945- )** and **Millett (1934- )** are artefacts of a 2009 text; the closed forms are **(1945-2012)** and **(1934-2017)** | L11, L12 |
| 75 | Treating any claim in the 🔶 lessons as the chapter's | ❌ Three tiers: chapter (cite by page), owner's extension (🔶, never cited to Gauba), outside both (declare) | L24 |

# MASTER COMPARISON, CAUSAL AND ARGUMENT MAPS

## Map 1 — The four schools on the questions that discriminate

| | **Liberal** | **Radical** | **Socialist** | 🔶 **Marxist** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Named in the chapter?** | ✅ Yes, with **no** thinker | ✅ Yes — **Firestone**, **Millett** | ✅ Yes — **Rowbotham** | ❌ No; 🔶 owner's extension |
| **Where the obstacle is** | Formal barriers and **gender-based discrimination** | Patriarchal sexual power; **ultimately biological** dependence | **Patriarchy and capitalism** together | The **capitalist mode of production**, **principally** |
| **Who benefits** | ⚠️ Not posed as a beneficiary question | Men | **Capitalists as well as men** | Capital |
| **Core claim** | **Absolute equality of opportunity … in all walks of life** | Firestone: irreducible, with a **hitherto inevitable** dependence now ended. Millett: **based on power and further sustained by an ideology** | **A combination of patriarchal analysis of radical feminism and class analysis of Marxism** | Oppression generated by **unpaid domestic/reproductive labour that reproduces the workforce free of charge** |
| **Characteristic programme** | **Equal pay for equal work, abortion laws reform, increasing representation** | **Diffusing the child-bearing and child-rearing role to society as a whole**; **smash these implements** | **Analysis of labour, both wage-labour and domestic labour**; **linking of the struggles of all oppressed groups** | The **domestic labour debate**; social reproduction |
| **Chapter's verdict** | **The most popular stream … but it is not considered to be very influential** | **The two pillars of radical feminism**, with **enormous influence** on the movement | Success **only through combining these struggles** | ⚠️ None — the chapter does not name it |
| ⚠️ **Standing limit** | Its programme reaches past its own principle | Chronological priority ≠ causal independence | The Rowbotham paragraph tilts toward class | **A pure class-reduction cannot explain patriarchal violence or control of sexuality that predates and outlasts capitalism** |

*Caption: the single highest-yield page in this file. Four of the five commonest errors in this topic are dating errors, and all four are prevented by reading this map once before writing.*

# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

⚠️ Bold here marks emphasis and key terms. Phrases carried from the source are reproduced as taught in the lessons; the lesson bodies and the blockquotes remain the place where exact source wording is presented as such. 🔶 marks material with no anchor in PDF pp.84-93.

## 1. Feminism and patriarchy

- ✅ Feminism **stands for the concern with the status and role of women in society in relation to men** — relational, not sectional.
- ✅ Women **have suffered and are still suffering injustice because of their sex**; hence feminism **seeks effective measures for the redressal of that injustice**.
- ✅ It is **a voice of protest against the inferior status accorded to women in society, which is the product of the institution of 'patriarchy' and not based on reason**.
- ⚠️ Four separable claims: relational / ground-in-sex / remedy-internal / cause-institutional-and-irrational. The fourth does two jobs — **institution** makes it alterable, **not based on reason** removes its justification.
- ⚠️ Sex is the **marker** by which victims are selected, not the **source**; the source named is patriarchy.
- ✅ Patriarchy box: the term **literally means 'rule of the father'**; originally **a social system based on the authority of male head of the household**; **now** **male domination in general, including its occurrence in labour market as well as domestic division of labour**.
- ⚠️ The widening is the analytical event — it moves patriarchy from family sociology into political theory.
- ❌ Mechanism, reproduction and historical cause are not supplied on p.84.

## 2. Early feminism

- ✅ **Early feminism emerged in the wake of Enlightenment, which sought to enlarge the scope of 'rights of man' so as to include equal rights to women therein** — an **extension** argument.
- ⚠️ Extension arguments are hard to resist because the premise is already conceded; the only resistance left is to deny women's rationality, which is where both Wollstonecraft and Mill aim.
- ✅ Enlightenment box: **an intellectual movement of eighteenth century France, Germany and Great Britain**; life **set free from obscure and orthodox beliefs**; **a new outlook, informed by reason and power of scientific research and discovery**; **old superstitions were discarded, old fears were dispelled**.
- ✅ **Mary Wollstonecraft (1759-97)** and **Harriet Taylor (1807-59)** — **free-thinking women** who **made a fervent appeal for equal rights of women**. ⚠️ Chapter's dates for Taylor; standard **1807-1858**.
- ✅ Wollstonecraft, *Vindication of the Rights of Woman* (**1792**): **women, like men, are rational individuals and should have equal rights**; she **established the principles on which campaigns for women's right to education, employment, property and the vote were later built up**.
- ✅ **John Stuart Mill (1806-73)**, *The Subjection of Women* (**1869**): **women were by no means less talented than men and hence deserved equal rights with men**; at p.88 he **sought to demonstrate** and **pleaded**.
- ❌ Harriet Taylor is given dates and nothing else — no work, no argument, no further mention.
- ✅ The transition: the status question is now **understood by drawing a distinction between sex and gender**.

## 3. Sex and gender

- ✅ **Sex**: **its scope is limited to biological differences such as reproductive function and secondary characteristics such as body hair and breast development** — defined by a **limit**.
- ✅ **Gender**: **cultural ideas that construct images and expectations of both females and males** — defined by what it **constructs**, and covering **both** sexes.
- ✅ Hinge: **nature has divided human race between men and women, but their status and role in society are determined by our culture**. ⚠️ Concedes biology, divides the question.
- ✅ Culture box: **values, beliefs, symbols, modes of thought and behaviour, styles of art and various skills evolved by the members of a group in order to streamline its social life**, establishing **a distinctive identity**. ⚠️ Authors ⇒ alterable; function ⇒ usable as control.
- ✅ The test: **'fair sex'**, **'weaker sex'**, **'ladies first'** — **our attention is not confined to the biological fact, but have already entered the realm of culture**. ⚠️ All three are flattering or polite.
- ✅ Social-science usage: **the differences in social status of man and woman**, specifically that **women are placed in a lower status in relation to their intrinsic worth**. ⚠️ **Intrinsic worth** supplies the normative standard.
- ✅ Remedy named here: **cultural transformation of society** and **the right ordering of status** — ⚠️ not a legal remedy.

## 4. Masculinity, femininity and the mechanism

- ✅ **Some typical attitudes towards gender can be found throughout the civilized world** — ⚠️ a puzzle, since culture usually produces difference; resolved by common **structures**.
- ✅ **Masculinity**: **aggressiveness, logical outlook, control of emotional expression, and attitude of dominance**. ✅ **Femininity**: **peacefulness, intuitiveness, emotional expressiveness, and submissiveness**. ✅ **Two opposite patterns.**
- ⚠️ Four pairs. **Logical outlook / intuitiveness** places women on the wrong side of the rationality criterion that grants rights; **dominance / submissiveness** is the only overtly hierarchical pair.
- ✅ Concession: **a wife may be relatively submissive to her husband, but as a mother she may not be so towards her children**; degree **may vary from one case to another**. ⚠️ Submissiveness is **positional**.
- ✅ Surviving claim: **relative dominance of man and relative submissiveness of woman represent almost universal cultural traits, which are not directly based on biological differences** — ⚠️ keep all three hedges.
- ✅ Three causes: **patriarchy and its institutions**, **the division of labour in the family**, **the competitive and exploitative character of capitalism**. ❌ The chapter does not connect them to the three schools.
- ✅ **The concepts of masculinity and femininity serve as instruments of social control that reinforce male dominance.**
- ✅ Enforcement by re-description: authoritarian behaviour by a woman **is termed to be indecent**.
- ✅ **The expectations attached to differential roles of men and women serve as the foundation of gender inequality in society.**

## 5. Rousseau, and the chart

- ✅ **J.J. Rousseau (1712-78)**, *A Discourse on the Origin of Inequality* (**1755**).
- ✅ **Natural inequality**: **age, health, beauty, physical and intellectual capacities**, **created by nature**, **largely unalterable**.
- ✅ **Conventional inequality**: **wealth, prestige and power**, **the product of our social arrangements**, open to **a critical examination of these inequalities from the point of view of justice**, and **alterable**.
- ⚠️ **Power** sits in the conventional column, which licenses the representation argument four pages later.
- ✅ Application: the **division of society into two sexes** is natural; **gender inequalities are the product of convention and culture** and **can be questioned and removed wherever they are found objectionable**.
- ⚠️ Two steps: conventional → **assessable**; found objectionable → **removable**. Convention removes immunity; it does not convict.
- ⚠️ Tension to name: Rousseau's natural category is about differences of **degree**, while a division into two **kinds** is a classification; the chapter does not address this.
- ✅ Chart, under **Division of Society into Men and Women**: **Point of Division** Sex / Gender; **Basis** **Product of Nature** / **Product of Culture**; **Symptom** **Natural Inequality** / **Conventional Inequality**; **Character** **Unalterable** / **Alterable**.

## 6. Feminist theory, the 1980 report, and the biology argument

- ✅ Feminism or feminist theory **implies the advocacy of equal rights for women and men, accompanied by the commitment to improve the position of women in society**; it **seeks restoration of justice** for women deprived **since earliest times**.
- ⚠️ The second component is substantive, not formal; **restoration** implies something owed and withheld.
- 📰 **United Nations Report, 1980**, as quoted: women are **half the world's population**, perform **nearly two thirds of its work hours**, receive **one tenth of the world's income**, own **less than one hundredth of the world's property**.
- ❌ Never current. ❌ Never repeat **the condition remains more or less unchanged today** — the **today** is the 2009 edition's and no evidence is given.
- ⚠️ Safe use: the **shape** of the ratios — share falling steeply from work performed → income received → property owned — with the year attached.
- ✅ Thesis: **it has now been proved beyond doubt that biological differences between man and woman do not justify inferior status of women** — ⚠️ **proved beyond doubt** is the chapter's own confidence.
- ✅ The reversal: **on the contrary, cultural factors have contributed to accentuate the biological differences between men and women** ⇒ the justification is **circular**.
- ✅ Diachronic evidence: **the rapid improvement in women's athletic records in recent decades is an indication that social norms had shaped biology and restricted women's physical development**.
- ✅ Synchronic evidence: **feminist anthropologists have pointed out that in some ethnic groups physical differentiation between men and women is not as prominent as in others**. ❌ Nobody named in either.
- ✅ Conclusion: **the present status of women is chiefly the product of social arrangements** — ⚠️ keep **chiefly**.

## 7. Socialization

- ✅ **Women often spend most of their time in domestic work and in rearing children**; **most women do not get an opportunity to develop their own personality**.
- ✅ They **are made to believe that the proper sphere of their activity is within their household and that they need not take interest in public life** — ⚠️ belief, not prohibition; internal, needing no enforcement.
- ✅ **From the beginning girls are taught to pay more attention to personal relations, not to personal success.**
- ✅ **Boys are taught to be firm, assertive and aggressive; girls are taught to be obedient, shy and submissive.**
- ✅ **Boys are encouraged to become doctors, engineers and lawyers; girls are encouraged to become school teachers, nurses or secretaries.**
- ⚠️ Three levels — orientation, disposition, occupation — each reinforcing the next; the discriminator in the occupational lists is **autonomy and command**, not skill.
- ✅ **The experience gained by women in their own professional life does not help them to take up a political career.** ⚠️ This explains an **authority** gap, not a turnout gap. ❌ Asserted without evidence.

## 8. The historical account

- ✅ The chapter's own question: **if injustice against women has existed since earliest times, why has it come to the forefront only in recent times?**
- ✅ Stage 1: **biological differences between men and women necessitated the division of labour between them**; men **chose to go out for hunting and other hazardous jobs**; women **constrained to undertake child-bearing and rearing chose to remain at home**; the system was **based on mutual care and adjustment, and did not involve any significant level of resentment**. ❌ Not an anthropological finding. ⚠️ **Chose** sits uneasily with **necessitated**/**constrained**.
- ✅ Stage 2, the hinge: **with the development of technology, sweeping changes took place in other parts of social organization, but the division of labour between men and women remained more or less unchanged**.
- ✅ Stage 3: **man as head of the family, of the clan and of the tribe acquired more and more power but woman largely continued to hold the subordinate position** — ⚠️ the gap **widens**.
- ✅ Stage 4 compensations: **concessions and exemptions from strenuous and hazardous tasks** as the **'weaker sex'**; **not allowed to mix with strangers**; **shyness was eulogized as a woman's ornament**; beauty **admired in poetry, music, paintings and other works of art**; **'fair sex', endowed with special dignity**; **'ladies first'**; **'a damsel in distress'** and **chivalry**. ⚠️ All honorific, none redistributive.
- ✅ Verdict: **systematically deprived of her share in power**; **given security but not an opportunity to learn certain things that would make her as competent as man, and thereby vindicate her claim to equality**.
- ✅ Three deprivations: **the right to ownership of property**, **right to vote**, **opportunities of education and higher learning** — with **no logical connection with her biological status as a woman**.
- ⚠️ Second circularity: the deprivation manufactures the incapacity that justifies it.

## 9. The rights agenda

- ✅ In **1792**: **restrained from voting**, **deemed unfit for education**, **debarred from many occupations**, **no legal right to own property**, and **no real right to divorce even if her husband abused her**.
- ⚠️ The fifth is exclusion from **exit** from a private relation. ⚠️ Keep **no real right**.
- ✅ Nineteenth century: **removing educational and professional barriers**; the spirit **often quite militant**, **culminated in early twentieth century struggles for women's suffrage**. ❌ No organisation, country or date named.
- ✅ **More recent demands … in the western world**: **employment rights, equal pay and equality in social benefits, taxation and so on**.
- ⚠️ Drift: **access** → **terms** → **redistribution**.
- ✅ **It is now realized that women are fit to perform most of the jobs that men do**; **equal rights for women are no longer questioned in enlightened circles**. ⚠️ Keep **most of the jobs**, **in enlightened circles**, and the **2009** vantage.
- ⚠️ The credited driver is structural — **advancement in technology**, **diversification**, **the increasing demand of new skills**.

## 10. Liberal feminism

- ✅ **Feminist theory has evolved into various schools of thought. Of these three are particularly important: (a) Liberal feminism, (b) Radical feminism, and (c) Socialist feminism.** ⚠️ The chapter disclaims exhaustiveness in its own sentence.
- ✅ It **aims at the revival of the conventional feminist movement**.
- ✅ It **insists on absolute equality of opportunity for men and women in all walks of life and complete removal of gender-based discrimination in society**.
- ✅ Programme: **equal pay for equal work, abortion laws reform, increasing representation of women in parliaments, bureaucracy and dignified professions, etc.**
- ⚠️ Only the first is pure non-discrimination; abortion-law reform concerns a capacity only one sex has, and **increasing representation** names an outcome.
- ✅ Verdict: **the most popular stream of feminist movement, but it is not considered to be very influential** — ⚠️ passive voice, no source, no argument.
- ❌ The chapter names **no** liberal-feminist thinker.

## 11. Radical feminism — Firestone

- ✅ **Shulamith Firestone**, *The Dialectic of Sex* (**1970**), **its chief spokesperson** — ⚠️ chapter prints **(1945- )**; standard **(1945-2012)**.
- ✅ Irreducibility: subordination **could not be understood as a symptom or aspect of some deeper or more comprehensive system of domination, such as racism or class-based division of society**.
- ✅ Priority: **historically women constituted the first oppressed group**; subordination **could not be eliminated by the changes such as the elimination of prejudice or even the abolition of class society**.
- ⚠️ "Radical" = **root-level**. ⚠️ Chronological priority does not establish present causal independence.
- ✅ Basis: **the basis of women's subordination was ultimately biological**; **human reproductive biology was responsible for considering women the weaker sex**.
- ✅ Chain: **infants should depend on lactating women and women in turn, should depend on men**. ⚠️ Link 1 biological, link 2 a conditional social arrangement.
- ✅ Exit: **reliable contraceptives, baby foods and 'test-tube babies'** gave **the means of freeing themselves from the tyranny of their reproductive biology** and of **diffusing the child-bearing and child-rearing role to society as a whole, men as well as women**. ⚠️ **Hitherto inevitable** marks the end of the inevitability.

## 12. Radical feminism — Millett

- ✅ **Kate Millett**, *Sexual Politics* — chapter dates **(1971)**; ⚠️ verified standard first publication **1970**. Chapter prints **(1934- )**; standard **(1934-2017)**.
- ✅ **The relationship between the sexes was based on power and further sustained by an ideology.** ⚠️ Power gives substance, ideology gives durability.
- ✅ **It was similar to the relationship between classes and races.** ⚠️ **Similar to** ≠ **reducible to** — no conflict with Firestone.
- ✅ **Hence it should be treated as political relationship.** ❌ Do not attribute the slogan "the personal is political" to this page.
- ✅ **Basing her analysis … on Max Weber's theory of domination**, domination is exercised **in two forms: through social authority and economic force**. ❌ No Weberian typology is supplied. ➡️ Power and authority owner.
- ✅ **Time had now come to smash these implements of man's domination** — ⚠️ **implements** implies tools, hence a user and a possible removal.
- ✅ **Firestone and Millett are regarded to be the two pillars of radical feminism**, with **enormous influence on developing the Women's Liberation Movement in 1970s**. ⚠️ Firestone answers **why**, Millett answers **what**.

## 13. Socialist feminism

- ✅ **A combination of patriarchal analysis of radical feminism and class analysis of Marxism.**
- ✅ **Capitalists as well as men are the beneficiary of women's subordination** — ⚠️ two beneficiaries ⇒ two systems ⇒ dual-system, blocking reduction in both directions.
- ✅ **Analysis of labour, both wage-labour and domestic labour** — ⚠️ counting domestic work as labour brings the household into political economy.
- ✅ They **have also considered the role of culture and psycho-analytical aspects of sexuality** — ❌ so the school is not economics-only; ⚠️ no further detail is given on either strand.
- ✅ **Sheila Rowbotham**, **the chief representative** — ⚠️ chapter prints **Rowbatham**.
- ✅ Method: **a participatory, decentralized approach to social change that contemplates linking of the struggles of all oppressed groups** — ⚠️ **linking**, not ranking.
- ✅ Works: ***Women, Resistance and Revolution* (1972)**, ***Hidden from History* (1973)**, ***The Past is before Us* (1989)** — **her best-known historical writings**, which **tried to reclaim the past for women as a source of knowledge and strength that could contribute to their present struggle**.
- ✅ **As a Marxist**, women's liberation is **essentially bound with the struggle against capitalism**; **class exploitation and women's oppression are closely linked phenomena**; success **only through combining these struggles**.
- ⚠️ Unresolved tension between the symmetric opening and the class-tilted Rowbotham paragraph.

## 14. The Women's Liberation Movement

- ✅ Emerged in the **United States** since the **early 1970s**; **soon … also spread to Europe and other parts of the world**; prompted by awareness of exploitation on **social, cultural, political and economic fronts**.
- ✅ Six organisations: **National Organization for Women (NOW)**, **Boston's Bread and Roses**, **Berkley Women's Liberation Group**, **Women's Radical Action Project**, **Women's Equity Action League (WEAL)**, **National Women's Political Caucus (NWPC)**. ⚠️ Chapter prints **Berkley**; standard **Berkeley**. ❌ No dates, leaders or programmes.
- ✅ Core demand: **a truly equal treatment of men and women**, requiring that **many of society's myths, values and beliefs concerning status and role of women in society should be fundamentally reassessed and changed**.
- ✅ Domains: **work and family life, social behaviour, decision-making, politics, religion and education**; and **even the more personal and private domain of sexuality needed to be redefined**. ⚠️ **Even** marks the boundary case.
- ✅ Three debated issues: **day-care facilities for children**; **the development of a non-sexist vocabulary (e.g. the term 'chairman' should be replaced by 'chairperson')**; **the representation of women and their roles in the mass media, including advertising**.
- ✅ Outcomes: **the enactment of new laws in some countries**, and **the worldwide acceptance of new norms protecting the dignity of women**. ⚠️ Note the asymmetry of scope.

## 15. The Indian material inside the chapter

- ✅ **Protection** statutes: **Immoral Traffic (Prevention) Act, 1956**; **Dowry Prohibition Act, 1961**; **Indecent Representation of Women (Prohibition) Act, 1986**; **Commission of Sati (Prevention) Act, 1987**.
- ✅ Female foeticide: **Prenatal Diagnostic Techniques (Regulation and Prevention of Misuse) Act, 1994**.
- ✅ **Empowerment** statutes: **Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act, 2005**, **which gives equal right to daughters in joint family property**; **Protection of Women from Domestic Violence Act, 2005**.
- ⚠️ The chapter's own **protection / empowerment** sort maps onto Lesson 8's **security but not an opportunity**. ⚠️ The 2005 succession amendment addresses the first named deprivation.
- ✅ **Until the condition of equal participation of women in public life is fulfilled, the concept of citizenship cannot be brought to its logical conclusion** — a claim about conceptual **coherence**.
- ✅ **A beginning … reservation of one-third of the seats in panchayats**, encouraging women **to join politics at grass-root level**.
- ❌ Projections — **increased to one-half**, **legislative assemblies and parliament**, **higher education** leading to **administration and high-profile professions** — are **2009 expectations**, never achievements.
- ❌ No amendment number, article, legislative history, repeal, judgment or post-2009 statute. ➡️ Polity owner.

## 16. After rights: the participation puzzle

- ✅ **Even after getting full citizenship in law, women continue to suffer from subjection in their social life.**
- ✅ **From 1960s and 1970s the status of women in society and politics has become the centre of attention.**
- ✅ The defeated assumption: **earlier it was usually thought that after establishing legal equality of men and women, women were not left with any issue of complaint**. ⚠️ Valid on a formal conception of equality — hence the limit of liberal feminism.
- ✅ **There were some studies on voting behaviour**; **women's participation in voting was lower in comparison to men**. ❌ No study, country, date or figure named.
- ✅ Explanation 1: **largely interested in private and domestic affairs**, **less interested in politics and public affairs**, **probably they had no time**. ⚠️ **Probably** is the chapter's hedge.
- ✅ Invalidation: **the size of the family began to shrink** and **more and more women took up jobs**, so it **was no longer held to be valid**.
- ✅ Residue: **more and more women were taking part in voting, but their share at various levels of political authority had remained insignificant**. ⚠️ The problem moves from participation to **authority**.

## 17. The representation evidence

- ❌ All figures are late-twentieth-century illustrations in a **2009** edition.
- ✅ Electorates **nearly fifty per cent women**; representation **far below that of men**. ✅ Western Europe **less than ten per cent**; **British House of Commons** **less than five per cent**; **United States House of Representatives** **not very different** — ⚠️ no US figure given.
- ✅ Exception: **Sweden and Denmark** **twenty-six per cent**; **Norway** **thirty-four per cent**; the situation there **was not that bad**. ⚠️ The decisive logical point — the gap is **contingent**. ❌ No explanation of why is given.
- ✅ **November 1990**, **thirty-four Heads of Government**, the **Charter of Paris for the New Europe** ⚠️ (official form: **for a New Europe**), marking **the end of the Cold War**, headlined **'end of an era'**; **but feminists ask: "Which era had come to an end?"**; **it was not an end of the patriarchal era**.
- ✅ **Only two women could be spotted after a thorough search**: **Gro Brundtland** (Norway) and **Margaret Thatcher** (Britain); **two days after this Conference, Mrs. Thatcher also resigned, and a man replaced her**.
- ✅ The thesis question: **what is the consequence of granting full political rights to women if their representation in public life remains so negligible?**
- ✅ Qualification: **by the end of 2005** Germany's first woman Chancellor; **by the beginning of 2006** Chile and Liberia; **in 2007** India and Argentina; and **Sri Lanka, Israel, India, Pakistan and Bangladesh** have had women Prime Ministers or Heads of Government, though **the number of women holding high offices is very small**.
- ✅ The discriminator: such records are **enough proof of the potential of women's power. But it is no proof of the opportunities open to women as their share in these positions continues to be very meagre.**

## 18. The two views on equality

- ✅ View (a): **there is no difference between men and women as regards their capabilities; hence they should be governed by the same laws**.
- ✅ View (b): **women are essentially different from men—biologically, culturally and socially; they should be given equal opportunities to develop and apply their distinctive capabilities along with equal rights**.
- ⚠️ View (b) is not the condemned essentialism — its difference is **culturally and socially** constituted too, and **equal rights** are retained.
- ✅ Four kinds of measure: **exemption** (**hazardous tasks, like underground mining and working in night shifts**); **provision** (**maternity leave and related benefits**, **maintenance and custody of children after divorce**); **compensation** (**reservations … in the seats for higher learning, appointments, seats in legislatures**, to offset **under-representation in important positions**); **incentive** (**tax concessions … to encourage them to work for additional income**).
- ⚠️ They rest on different arguments; **exemption** narrows opportunity and sits against Lesson 9's barrier-removal agenda.
- ✅ Verdict: **this view seems to be more reasonable and is widely endorsed** — ⚠️ hedged, with **no** supporting argument.

## 19. Human Development Report 1995

- ❌ Everything here is the **Human Development Report, 1995**, reported in a **2009** book.
- ✅ **GDI** — **gender-related development index**, **for a group of 130 countries (out of a total of 174 countries included in the report)**. ✅ **GEM** — **gender empowerment measure**, **the extent to which women participate in a country's economic and political life**.
- ⚠️ GDI : GEM :: provision : power :: **security** : **opportunity** :: **protection** : **empowerment**.
- ✅ Top: **Sweden, Finland, Norway and Denmark**, indicating **the virtual absence of any gender bias in their development process**. ✅ Bottom: **scores under 0.3 (compared to a maximum possible value of 1.00)**, **mostly African or Islamic nations** — ❌ never generalise or treat as causal. ✅ **India ranks 99 in terms of GDI of the 130 countries.**
- ✅ Quoted: **moving towards gender equality is not a technocratic goal—it is a political process … it requires a new way of thinking—in which the stereotyping of women and men gives way to a new philosophy that regards all people, irrespective of gender, as essential agents of change**.
- ✅ Finding 1: **the report does not find any correlation between gender bias and a country's economic development** — Cuba, **72 on Human Development Index (of the 174 countries)**, **47 on the GDI** and **16 on the GEM (of the 130 countries)**.
- ✅ Quoted: **countries applying socialist models used social and political mobilization to achieve rapid and equal progress in education and health for women and men and to engineer social transformations to expand opportunities for women** — ⚠️ a **GDI**-dimension claim only.
- ✅ Finding 2: **no essential correlation between GDI and GEM in many cases** — **adequate attention to health care and education of women, but no adequate share in the exercise of power**; the **'economic tigers' of East Asia**, where **in spite of substantial improvement in the level of development of women, they have been denied a tangible share in economic and political power, in an essentially male-dominated society**.
- ✅ Closing: feminist justice **calls for securing the overall development of women, including improvement of their health and education as well as giving them adequate share in economic and political power** — ⚠️ both limbs.

## 20. 🔶 Provenance of the distinction

- 🔶 ⚠️ Outside PDF pp.84-93. The owner states that Gauba **does not discuss** Beauvoir, Oakley, Rubin, Pateman, Okin, Butler or Crenshaw.
- 🔶 **Simone de Beauvoir**, *The Second Sex*, **1949** (verified) — the **philosophical root**: **womanhood is a social becoming rather than a fixed biological given**. ❌ Paraphrase only.
- 🔶 **Ann Oakley**, *Sex, Gender and Society*, **1972** — **usually credited with popularising** the terminology; gender as **culturally learned, historically variable**. ⚠️ Keep both hedges.
- 🔶 **Gayle Rubin**, "The Traffic in Women", **1975** — the **sex/gender system**: arrangements that **transform biological sex into a hierarchy of gendered obligation, kinship exchange and constrained sexuality**; she **explains how sex becomes gender, which Gauba's chapter asserts but does not mechanise**.
- ❌ Mandatory caveat: **do not present a single, uncontested "origin story" as settled fact — the distinction has multiple co-formulators and was consolidated gradually; avoid taxonomic overclaim**.

## 21. 🔶 Marxist feminism, Pateman, Okin

- 🔶 **Marxist feminism** — claim: oppression generated **principally by the capitalist mode of production, especially unpaid domestic/reproductive labour that reproduces the workforce free of charge**; evidence: the **1970s "domestic labour debate"**; significance: relocates the household **inside political economy**; limit: **a pure class-reduction cannot explain patriarchal violence or control of sexuality that predates and outlasts capitalism**.
- 🔶 ✅ Exam rule: **Marxist** → domestic labour / social reproduction; **socialist** → Rowbotham's dual systems; **the two labels overlap but are not simple synonyms**.
- 🔶 **Carole Pateman**, *The Sexual Contract* (**1988**) — a **prior "sexual contract"** securing **men's access to women's bodies and labour**; narratives are **silent on the marriage contract**; it reframes **liberal equality as resting on an unexamined patriarchal foundation**. ❌ **An interpretive lens, not a documented historical event.**
- 🔶 **Susan Moller Okin**, *Justice, Gender, and the Family* (**1989**) — justice theories, targeting **Rawls**, treat **the family as a pre-political given**; the **unequal domestic burden restricts women's access to the "primary goods" (time, income, position)**; it **extends justice from state/market into the household**. ⚠️ Limit: risks **over-extending justice into every private relationship**.
- ➡️ Contract theory to its own owner; Rawls to the justice owner.

## 22. 🔶 Butler, Crenshaw, and the two chains

- 🔶 **Judith Butler**, *Gender Trouble* (**1990**): gender is **performative**, **produced through repeated, stylised acts (speech, gesture, dress, behaviour) that create the appearance of a natural gendered self**; **even "sex" is partly constituted through gendered discourse**.
- ❌ Three use-conditions: not Gauba's position; not a denial that bodies exist (the claim is about **the meaning and categorisation of bodies**); always paired with Chain 1.
- 🔶 **Kimberlé Crenshaw**, "Demarginalizing the Intersection of Race and Sex" (**1989**): **treating race and sex discrimination as separate, additive categories fails women who face a distinct, compounded disadvantage at their intersection**; claims fall **through the gap between a race-discrimination template and a sex-discrimination template, each implicitly modelled on a different unmarked group**; it corrects **an unstated universalism**. ⚠️ Limit: it **supplements, but should not replace, the structural claims** of the schools.
- 🔶 **Chain 1:** objection — performativity costs feminism **a stable category of "woman"**; reply — **provisionally, strategically stabilised categories** and the pragmatic functioning of legal categories. ⚠️ **A live, contested debate — not a settled result.**
- 🔶 **Chain 2:** objection — near-universal patriarchy risks **treating "women" as one undifferentiated class, erasing differences of race, class and caste**; reply — intersectionality and class attention, oppression **compounded and differently configured across axes**. ⚠️ It **strengthens rather than replaces** the radical insight.

## 23. 🔶 Indian application, and the declared absences

- 🔶 **Savitribai Phule** — **widely associated, with Jyotirao Phule, with pioneering girls' education in nineteenth-century Maharashtra**; ceiling: **early advocate of women's education against caste-and-gender exclusion**. ❌ No school-opening dates or numbers.
- 🔶 **B. R. Ambedkar** — **widely associated with linking women's legal and social equality to the wider project of social democracy, including reform of Hindu personal law**; treat as **a general, well-known orientation**. ❌ No bill provisions, sections or dates.
- 🔶 **The Indian women's movement**, **from the 1970s onward** — a standard anchor for **formal legal equality is not the end of the feminist question**; use **as a type of example (movement-led legal reform)**. ❌ No statute names, years or case citations.
- 🔶 Governing rule: this topic owns the **conceptual** application and **does not own specific constitutional articles, acts or case law**. ❌ And it is **not** a current-data note.
- ❌ **Engels** (*The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State*, **1884**) and **Friedan** (*The Feminine Mystique*, **1963**) — titles and years verified, but **neither is named** in the chapter or either owner. ⚠️ Declare the absence; attribute no claim.

## 24. Separating the schools, and answer architecture

- ⚠️ Four discriminating questions: **where is the obstacle**; **who benefits**; **what counts as success**; 🔶 **which women**.
- ⚠️ Four standing checks: 📰 **date**; 🔶 **provenance** (chapter / owner's extension / outside both); ❌ **boundary** (no article, section, case, amendment or current figure); ⚠️ **homogeneity**.
- ⚠️ 10 marks: thesis → sex/gender with the hinge → one stream → one limit → one-line conclusion.
- ⚠️ 15 marks: thesis → doctrine → two or three streams as claim/evidence/significance/**limit** → one objection-reply chain → one hedged Indian illustration → reasoned conclusion.
- ⚠️ 20 marks: thesis + directive decoding → full doctrine with Rousseau → all streams including 🔶 Marxist → 🔶 Pateman/Okin where relevant → **both** chains → hedged Indian application → **graded** conclusion.
- ⚠️ Directives: **Discuss/Examine** needs diagnosis **and** reply; **Distinguish** needs the distinction **plus why it matters**; **Critically examine** needs thesis + two schools + objection-reply + verdict.
- ⚠️ Quotation method: **paraphrased, named propositions over unverified verbatim quotations** unless independently confirmed.
- ⚠️ Closing thesis: the argument about **rights** was won; the distribution of **power** was not — three goods delivered without power following.

- The local PDF's page numbering runs **ahead** of the printed page numbering, and the offset is **not constant across the book**.
- **This topic's section**: PDF pp.**84-93** = printed pp.**70-79** — offset **14**.
- Every citation in this file gives the **PDF** page with the printed page in parentheses where both are stated. ⚠️ No universal offset should be inferred from this figure; sibling topics in this subject use a different offset for other chapters.

| ⚠️ | Inference, caution, unresolved tension, or a limit on what the evidence supports |

| 📰 | A dated anchor, carried with its date attached |

## Open uncertainties

1. ⚠️ ***Sexual Politics* date.** The chapter prints **(1971)**. An independent check in this session confirms the standard first publication as **1970** (Doubleday). The chapter's date stands where its wording is reproduced; **1970** is used in this session's own voice, and the divergence is disclosed at Lesson 12 and in Remediation entry 73.
2. ⚠️ **Open life-dates.** The chapter prints **Shulamith Firestone (1945- )**, **Kate Millett (1934- )** and **Sheila Rowbotham (1943- )**. Firestone died in **2012** and Millett in **2017**, both independently verified in this session. The open forms are artefacts of a 2009 text and are reproduced only when the chapter's wording is being reproduced.
3. ⚠️ **Millett's surname.** The chapter prints **Millett** when introducing her and **Millet** four lines later in the Weber sentence. Normalised to **Millett** and catalogued.
4. ⚠️ **Rowbotham's surname.** The chapter prints **Rowbatham** consistently; the standard form, used by both canonical owners, is **Rowbotham**. This is a divergence from the standard reference rather than between the local sources, and the standard form is used here.
5. ⚠️ **Berkeley.** The chapter prints **Berkley Women's Liberation Group**; the place name is standardly **Berkeley**. The chapter's form is reproduced where its wording is reproduced, and the variance is recorded.
6. ⚠️ **Statute short titles.** The chapter gives short titles and years only. Where a short title has a documented history — the 1956 Act was originally enacted under a different name and renamed by a later amendment, and the 1994 Act is commonly cited with a hyphenated "Pre-natal" and was itself amended afterwards — the chapter records none of it, and none is asserted here. ➡️ Polity owner.
7. ⚠️ **The "proved beyond doubt" claim.** The chapter's confidence that **biological differences … do not justify inferior status of women** has been **proved beyond doubt** is its own assertion, and the two illustrations supplied immediately after it — athletic records and feminist anthropologists — name no sport, figure, study, scholar or group. Reproduced as the chapter's assertion, not as a demonstrated result.
8. ⚠️ **The continuity sentence.** **The condition remains more or less unchanged today** is asserted in the chapter's own voice with no evidence, and its **today** is the Fifth Edition's. It is reproduced only to be marked as unusable.
9. ⚠️ **Unsourced empirical reports.** The voting-behaviour finding is attributed only to **some studies**, with no author, country, date or figure; the **United States House of Representatives** is described only as **not very different**, with no percentage; and the Scandinavian exception is given without any explanation of its cause. Each limit is stated at the point of use.
10. ⚠️ **The chapter's category assignment for the sexual division.** It places the **division of society into two sexes** in Rousseau's **natural inequality** column, although Rousseau's category as the chapter itself defines it concerns differences of **degree** — **age, health, beauty, physical and intellectual capacities** — while a division into two kinds is a classification rather than a ranking. The chapter does not address this, and the tension is taught rather than smoothed.
11. ⚠️ **Choice versus necessity at p.87.** The historical account says that biological differences **necessitated** the division of labour and that women were **constrained** to undertake child-bearing, while also saying that men and women **chose** their respective spheres. The chapter does not reconcile the vocabulary.
12. ⚠️ **Exemption versus barrier-removal.** Lesson 18's second view proposes exempting women from **hazardous tasks, like underground mining and working in night shifts**, which narrows the occupations open to them — the opposite of the nineteenth-century agenda of **removing educational and professional barriers** that the chapter endorses at Lesson 9. The chapter endorses the view without noticing the tension.
13. ⚠️ **The endorsement without an argument.** **This view seems to be more reasonable and is widely endorsed** is hedged twice and supported by no reasoning anywhere in the chapter. Any justification offered for it in this session is labelled as a reconstruction.
14. ⚠️ **The 1995 report's bottom-of-table characterisation.** **Mostly African or Islamic nations** groups a continent with a religious category and is reproduced strictly as a dated report of a dated index, never as a causal or regional claim. The chapter's own Finding 1 — no correlation between gender bias and economic development — undercuts a simple reading of the ranking.
15. ⚠️ **The Charter of Paris particulars, and its title.** The date (**November 1990**), the offices held by **Gro Brundtland** and **Margaret Thatcher** at that date and Thatcher's resignation within days are consistent with standard references. ⚠️ The **title**, however, diverges: the chapter prints **Charter of Paris for the New Europe**, while the official instrument adopted at the Paris summit of the Conference on Security and Co-operation in Europe is the **Charter of Paris for a New Europe** — **a**, not **the**. The chapter's form is preserved where its wording is reproduced and the official form is used in this session's own voice. The chapter's count of **thirty-four** heads of government and its claim about the group photograph are reproduced as the chapter's and were **not** independently verified in this pass.
16. ⚠️ **Harriet Taylor's dates.** The chapter prints **(1807-59)**; an independent check in this session gives the standard dates as **1807-1858**, so the chapter's closing year is one later than the standard reference. The chapter's form is preserved where its wording is reproduced; the standard form is recommended in a candidate's own voice. ⚠️ Nothing in the chapter's argument turns on the date, since it attaches no work or thesis to her.
17. ⚠️ **Secondary PYQ numbering.** The four secondary candidates named for transparency were re-verified against each year's printed Section A structure, in which **Q1** carries five parts of **10** marks and **Q2-Q4** the **15-** and **20-**mark parts. The verified numbering is **2023 Q1(c) [10]**, **2021 Q1(e) [10]**, **2019 Q1(e) [10]** and **2018 Q4(b) [15]**; three of the four sit in the Q1 short-answer block. The nine primary anchors were re-verified in the same pass and are unchanged.
18. ⚠️ **Edition and date discipline, with its scope stated precisely.** All substantive claims come from the **Fifth Edition, 2009**. The 1980 United Nations figures, the legislature percentages, the Charter of Paris illustration, the Human Development Report of **1995**, the Indian statutes described as **recent**, the women heads of state through **2007**, and every occurrence of **today**, **now**, **currently**, **recent** and **contemporary** in the chapter are **2009** statements and are not presented as current.

    ❌ **The prohibition covers substantive content:** no post-2009 **fact about the world**, **statistic**, **statute or amendment**, **judgment**, **policy, scheme or programme**, **index, ranking or report** is introduced anywhere in this session, and none is substituted for a dated figure.

    ⚠️ **Three classes of post-2009 information are expressly exempt from that prohibition, because they correct the record rather than extend it.** **(a) Verified bibliographic corrections** — the first publication of *Sexual Politics* as **1970** against the chapter's 1971, and the titles and years of *The Second Sex* (**1949**), *The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State* (**1884**) and *The Feminine Mystique* (**1963**). **(b) Verified life-date corrections** — **Firestone (1945-2012)** and **Millett (1934-2017)**, whose death dates necessarily postdate the edition and whose open forms in the chapter are artefacts of its date, together with **Harriet Taylor (1807-1858)** and the official form of the **Charter of Paris for a New Europe**. **(c) Examination years** — the Philosophy Optional papers of **2010-2026** and the search pass over the **2025** and **2026** General Studies papers, which are the exam record this session is written against rather than claims about the world. ⚠️ Each exemption is disclosed at the point of use and carried in these uncertainties; none supplies a substantive, statistical, statutory or policy claim.
19. ⚠️ **PYQ transcription spacing.** The Philosophy Optional stems are reproduced as the local ledgers carry them. A sibling topic in this subject established that the local 2018-2025 transcription sometimes closes up a printed space before a question mark; where the ledger preserves the space — the 2018 and 2026 stems — it is preserved here, and where it does not, the ledger wording stands and the uncertainty is recorded.
20. ⚠️ **2025 and 2026 General Studies papers.** The search pass covering reporting of those papers found no question naming feminism, patriarchy or the sex/gender distinction as a political-theory doctrine. That is a finding over reporting, not a line-by-line reading of the official texts.
21. ⚠️ **Extension provenance.** Lessons 20-23 and register §§20-23 are reconstructed from the subject owner's §§13-15 and carry **no** anchor in PDF pp.84-93. Only the bibliographic facts stated there are asserted, and three were independently verified in this session: *The Second Sex* (**1949**), *The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State* (**1884**) and *The Feminine Mystique* (**1963**). The owner's own caveats — against an **origin story**, against treating Pateman's thesis as **a documented historical event**, and against presenting Butler as Gauba's position or as a denial that bodies exist — are reproduced rather than summarised.
22. ⚠️ **Declared absences.** **Friedrich Engels** and **Betty Friedan** are named in **neither** the chapter nor either canonical owner. Their works and years are stated as verified bibliographic facts outside this topic's sources; **no** substantive claim is attributed to either from this topic.
23. ⚠️ **No official or current anchor was used in the lessons.** The verification pass described in the manifest confirmed bibliographic facts and searched the recent GS papers; it supplied no contemporary gender statistic, index, report, scheme or judgment, and none was forced into the teaching.

## Subject boundary

Political Theory is supplementary conceptual support for this subject stack; it is **not** a named owner of any General Studies paper. This file therefore claims no General Studies previous-year question and constructs no synthetic route to one. The Philosophy Optional stems used above are a **separate, explicitly labelled application source**, and ⚠️ unusually for this subject, **all nine are routed in the local ledgers to a different primary owner** — the Philosophy **Gender Discrimination** file — so listing them is a claim about what this topic **contributes**, never about what it owns.

The boundaries respected throughout: ➡️ constitutional articles, statutory sections, amendment numbers, legislative history and case law belong to the **Polity** owner; ➡️ female foeticide, sex ratios and the land-and-property evidence belong to the **Philosophy Gender Discrimination** owner; ➡️ the concept of citizenship belongs to the **citizenship** owner; ➡️ Rawls and the theory of justice belong to the **justice** owner; ➡️ Weber's typology of authority belongs to the **power and authority** owner; ➡️ the content of Marxism belongs to **Topic 04**; ➡️ the social-contract tradition belongs to its own owner, Rousseau appearing here only for the natural/conventional distinction. ❌ No Indian constitutional provision, government scheme, named public figure beyond those the chapter itself names, or post-2009 fact is asserted from this topic.
